---
id: work/mawkib-al-nour
lang: en
source_lang: ar
translators: []
---

## Summary
A daily Ramadan programme by Omar Abdelkafy titled "Mawkib al-Nour" (Procession of Light), pausing each day at one or two verses and drawing a lesson for real life, in long episodes of about forty minutes. This set holds episodes from the Assabile archive, and everything in it is the sheikh's speech as it stands in the machine transcript; what is not traced to a chain or is garbled in the transcript is marked as needing a source.

## Episode 1: introduction to the programme and writing down one's faults on the first day of Ramadan
- He says Ramadan is a chance for a person to stop with himself and review his accounts, and to see how he can build the earth with good so that people feel safe and fear and grief leave. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:02:00}
- He defines the programme as pausing each day at one or two verses of the Book of God and drawing a lesson to take down into real life, so that one lives not only in an ideal world but in a realistic idealism. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:03:30}
- He likens the Qur'an to the catalogue a maker puts with a device, the maker being God who knows what repairs and what spoils a human being, says the catalogue may not be altered by adding or taking away, and that the Qur'an contains fear, joy, grief, victory and defeat. This is his comparison. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:06:00}
- He relates by sense that the Prophet said "I have left among you what, if you hold to it, you will never go astray: the Book of God and my sunna". (needs_source: the report is by sense, without a chain or ascription) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:06:00}
- He says he does not deny psychiatry, but holds that many fears and social ills come from a flaw in the relation between creature and Creator, and likens the commands of religion to traffic signals, the green being the permitted things. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:07:00}
- He criticises someone who gains thirty kilograms in Ramadan and says he fasted, and holds that excess in the permitted is not good. See [[passage/quran-7-31|al-A'raf, verse 31]] and [[passage/quran-17-27|al-Isra, verse 27]]. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:08:00}
- He says Ramadan shows a smoker he can abstain for fifteen hours, so he should not make up at the breaking of the fast for what he abstained from. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:09:30}
- He relates by sense that the Prophet told Sa'd "make your food good and you will be answered when you pray", and that whoever eats a forbidden morsel has no deed accepted for forty days. (needs_source: both reports are by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:10:30}
- He says whoever eats doubtful food for forty days, his limbs disobey, and that backbiting, lying, tale-bearing and false witness are forbidden in the day and the night of Ramadan and all year. (needs_source: his claim about the forty days is his own without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:12:30}
- He reckons a lifetime, saying that of sixty years twenty go in sleep, fifteen in childhood, twenty in work and five on the phone. This is his rough sum. (needs_source: the calculation is his and has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:14:30}
- He says God chains the jinn devils in Ramadan and a person must chain the human devils, giving the example of someone with an immodestly dressed woman in his household. This is his opinion on dress, not the museum's judgement. (needs_source: the report on chaining the devils is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:15:30}
- He says that when acts of obedience increase, sleep decreases, that the people of Paradise do not sleep, and that disobedience is tied to much sleep and eating. (needs_source: his statement on the people of Paradise has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:16:30}
- He relates by sense that among the signs of the Hour is the nearing of time until a year is like a month and a month like a week, and blessing is removed from time. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:17:30}
- He says that whoever fears God is blessed in his wife, children and provision, quotes Ibn Abbas that he knew his sin in the bad temper of his mount and his wife, and cites the verse that God set right the wife of those who rushed to good deeds. See [[passage/quran-21-90|al-Anbiya, verse 90]]. (needs_source: the quotation from Ibn Abbas has no chain, and the name is garbled in reading) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:19:00}
- He says that praying the dawn prayer after sunrise takes away the blessing in provision, and that provision is divided between Muslim and unbeliever but blessing goes to the God-fearing. See [[passage/quran-11-111|Hud, verse 111]]. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:21:30}
- He calls on the viewer to keep a Ramadan notebook and write down his faults, the first being the word "I", and says a telecom company in New York counted five hundred calls in five minutes and found the word "I" 3,342 times. (needs_source: the statistic is his and has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:23:30}
- He holds that someone who says "I" three to five times in half an hour is mentally ill, and recalls that the first disobedience was the words "I am better than him". See [[passage/quran-7-12|al-A'raf, verse 12]]. This is his lay opinion, not a medical judgement. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:24:30}
- He advises looking in worldly matters at those who have less and in religion at those who are better, tells of a man who sold his sandals and then saw a legless man and thanked God, and tells the worried not to say "O Lord, I have a great worry" but "O worry, I have a great Lord". (needs_source: the story of the man who sold his sandals has no chain or name) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:28:30}
- He says the second fault is sins of the limbs and sins of the heart, and that if one gets rid of ten of thirty faults in Ramadan it is good. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:29:30}
- He counts conceit as the first sin of the heart, forbids handing out Paradise and Hell to people, and tells that Sa'id ibn Jubayr told al-Hajjaj, who wanted to kill him, that the unseen is known to another, and when al-Hajjaj threatened him with a blazing fire said "if it were up to you I would worship no one but you". (needs_source: the exchange between Sa'id and al-Hajjaj has no chain and its reading is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:32:30}
- He says the believer is pleased by his good deeds and grieved by his bad ones, that a person is a handful of earth and a breath of God's spirit, and relates of al-Ma'mun that he came to love pardon so much that he feared he would not be rewarded for it, citing the verses on those who restrain their anger. See [[passage/quran-3-133|Al Imran, verse 133]]. (needs_source: the report about al-Ma'mun has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:35:30}
- He says a little showing off is polytheism, and that the servant says his prayer, rites, living and dying are for God. See [[passage/quran-6-162|al-An'am, verse 162]]. (needs_source: the saying on a little showing off is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:37:00}
- He tells that Abd Allah ibn al-Mubarak would bear the costs of his pilgrimage companions, buy gifts for their families and then return their money, and says we can plant good according to our means once we are rid of conceit, pride, showing off and rancour. (needs_source: the story has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:39:30}
- He says that on seeing a blessing with another one should say "what God wills, blessed be God, no power but with God", and that the envier rejects the division of provision God made in eternity. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep01 00:41:00}

## Episode 2: counting one's faults, feeling secure from God's plan, and the tongue's sins
- He recalls what was agreed in the opening episode, that each person write his faults on a sheet, and says it is enough nobility for a person that his faults can be counted, likening it to the count of microbes in the blood that becomes an epidemic when it grows. This is his comparison. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:02:30}
- He tells that al-Hasan al-Basri saw people with a heap of date stones counting their glorifications and told them "count your sins, and I guarantee God will not lose your good deeds", and he criticises counters for glorification, saying the angels do not forget what a person utters. See [[passage/quran-50-18|Qaf, verse 18]]. (needs_source: the report of al-Hasan al-Basri has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:03:30}
- He likens writing the faults to a doctor's examination and a microscope lens, says one should put the "Qur'anic microscope" on one's heart and limbs, and that when you point a finger at others three fingers point back at you. This is his comparison. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:06:00}
- He says the Companions were busy with their own faults and had no time for others', whereas those after them did the opposite, and that whoever occupies his mind with others' faults kills himself with worry and falls into backbiting and tale-bearing. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:07:30}
- He counts feeling secure from God's plan among the diseases of the heart, and relates that Abu Bakr said that were one foot in Paradise and the other outside he would not feel secure from God's plan, and Umar that if a caller announced that all people were in Paradise but one he would fear he was the one. See [[passage/quran-23-60|al-Mu'minun, verse 60]]. (needs_source: the reports of Abu Bakr and Umar have no chain, and the reading is garbled in the answer given for Uthman) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:09:30}
- He counts despair of God's mercy as a major sin, says God's pardon is greater than sins, and explains "hayya 'ala al-salah" as the prayer being a lift that raises a person. This is his opinion on the wording. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:11:00}
- He relates by sense that a caller from beneath the Throne says "rise to your fires that you kindled against yourselves and put them out with prayer", and that whoever sins, performs ablution and prays two units without speaking to himself in them comes out of his sins. (needs_source: both reports are by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:13:00}
- He says the fasting person in Ramadan's day is like an angel, who does not eat, drink or tire, and that in Ramadan he is better with God than the angels if his side of good prevails, because God made the devil all evil, the angels all good and the human a mixture of both. This is his opinion. (needs_source: his statement that the human is better than the angels has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:15:30}
- He says that in Ramadan a person makes his heart a sky from which devils are struck by meteors if they approach. This is his comparison. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:16:30}
- He calls on viewers to write down the tongue's sins: backbiting, tale-bearing, lying, obscene speech, the binding false oath and false witness, and says the wounds of the spear heal but what the tongue wounds does not. (needs_source: the saying on wounds of the spear and tongue is not ascribed to anyone) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:18:00}
- He defines backbiting as mentioning your brother with what he dislikes, relates by sense the Prophet's saying that if it is in him you have backbitten and if not you have slandered him, and says it can concern the body, clothing, mount and character. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:20:00}
- He relates by sense that the backbiter, if he repents, is the last to enter Paradise and otherwise the first to enter the Fire, and that the bankrupt is one who comes with prayer and zakat but his good deeds are taken for those he insulted and backbit, and then their sins are cast on him. See [[passage/quran-11-111|Hud, verse 111]]. (needs_source: both reports are by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:21:30}
- He lists the steps of repenting from backbiting: regret, resolving not to return, and rebuilding what one demolished by mentioning the person well in the gatherings where one spoke ill of him and asking his pardon, and says the backbiter is a coward because he spoke behind someone's back. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:25:00}
- He relates of Ibn Umar that he said to the Kaaba how great is your sanctity, but the sanctity of the believer is greater with God, and says a believer's honour is dearer than the Kaaba's. (needs_source: the report about Ibn Umar has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:24:00}
- He says by sense that whoever's brother comes to him apologising and he does not accept his excuse, truthful or not, will not smell the fragrance of Paradise. (needs_source: the saying is by sense and without a reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:26:30}
- He tells the story of the man who killed ninety-nine people and then completed a hundred, asked a scholar whether he could repent and was told to move to a land of righteous people; he died on the way, the angels disputed over him, the earth was measured and he was found nearer to the land of obedience; from it he draws leaving the place of disobedience. (needs_source: the story is by sense, ascribed by him to the Prophet without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:29:00}
- He says God conceals sins and that if sins had a smell the whole earth would stink, and relates that Abu Bakr prayed that God not expose him before the witnesses. See [[passage/quran-14-34|Ibrahim, verse 34]]. (needs_source: the report of Abu Bakr's prayer has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:30:00}
- He likens repentance to a convalescence after surgery, and says the tongue that gossips about people's honour more deserves surgery than the appendix. This is his comparison. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:31:30}
- He relates that Aisha said to the Prophet of Safiyya that she is short, signing with her hand, and he said "you have said a word that, mixed with the sea, would have tainted it", and says a sign is backbiting too. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:32:30}
- He defines tale-bearing as carrying people's words to set them against one another, relates by sense that the Prophet said "let none report to me anything about my companions, for I want to come out to you with a sound heart", and cites those who go about with slander. See [[passage/quran-68-11|al-Qalam, verse 11]]. (needs_source: the reports are by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:34:30}
- He relates by sense that the Prophet told someone who reported another's sin "had you covered him with your garment it would have been better for you", and says do not deal with people as if you were a prophet and they unbelievers. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:35:30}
- He says that when a servant lies the angel of good deeds moves a mile away from the stench, and tells that Abu Bakr, asked about the Prophet on the road, said "this is a man who guides me on the way", and that the Prophet, asked who he was, said "from water", which the questioners took for a tribe. (needs_source: the account is without a chain, and the explanation of the double meaning is his) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:38:00}
- He forbids telling a child to say "tell your uncle I am asleep", says the liar is a cheat, and relates by sense that the Prophet passed a food seller whose grain had been wetted and said "whoever cheats us is not of us". (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:39:30}
- He says these first episodes rest on emptying oneself before adorning oneself, that is leaving sins and then entering the sweetness of faith. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep02 00:40:30}

## Episode 3: false witness, lowering the gaze and the moral spring
- He recalls that the last two episodes were on the faults of the tongue, backbiting, tale-bearing and lying, and says they tear the social fabric, since God made people peoples and tribes so as to cooperate. See [[passage/quran-49-13|al-Hujurat, verse 13]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:03:30}
- He holds that a frightened person cannot create or invent and that people need security, likening it to a sparrow and a cat, and criticises those who seize peoples' wealth, saying the cat is better than they because it runs off if it took what it should not. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:05:00}
- He tells that Abu Bakr's servant brought him milk, and after he drank the servant told him he had practised divination for a people in the Jahiliyya and they had given it to him, so Abu Bakr put his finger in his mouth and vomited. (needs_source: the story has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:06:30}
- He tells that Abu Hanifa as a baby was nursed by a neighbour's maid and his father Thabit made him bring up what he had drunk, that Abu Hanifa would say when he stumbled in a lesson that it might be from that milk, and quotes al-Shafi'i that people are dependants of Abu Hanifa in jurisprudence. (needs_source: the story and the quotation from al-Shafi'i have no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:09:00}
- He calls on those who work in charities to fear God over salaries and expenses, tells that Umar put out a lamp at the death of a Companion because the oil had become the heirs' right, and criticises extravagance in mourning ceremonies. (needs_source: the report about Umar has no chain or name) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:10:30}
- He says Muslims have prayed every Ramadan for God to return al-Aqsa Mosque for about sixty-six years, and holds that the prayer goes unanswered because their money has doubt and the forbidden in it, whereas answered prayer comes from lawful money. This is his political and religious opinion, not the museum's judgement. (needs_source: his link between money and unanswered prayer is his own opinion without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:12:00}
- He cites Moses' words to his servant that they had met fatigue on this journey after passing the place, and draws that tiredness comes when a person passes God's limits. See [[passage/quran-18-62|al-Kahf, verse 62]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:13:00}
- He calls on everyone to repent and starts with himself, citing the verse on repenting to God all together. See [[passage/quran-24-31|al-Nur, verse 31]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:15:00}
- He presents false witness, and says it includes testifying for one's kin, such as a daughter against her husband, before hearing both sides, and recalls the two litigants who came to David and that David judged before hearing the other. See [[passage/quran-38-22|Sad, verse 22]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:18:30}
- He tells that a man came to Shurayh weeping and complaining that his brothers had wronged him, and Shurayh said "how do you know", recalling that Joseph's brothers came to their father weeping at nightfall, and says the one who weeps is not always wronged. (needs_source: the report of Shurayh has no chain and its start is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:20:00}
- He criticises false praise of officials in poems as hypocrisy and says one should bear witness only to the truth. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:20:30}
- He repeats that these first days are for emptying oneself of sins before adorning oneself with prayer, Qur'an and supplication. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:21:00}
- He relates by sense that the Prophet said the nations will call to one another against you as diners call to a bowl, and that weakness (wahn) is love of life and hatred of death. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:22:00}
- He says a Muslim prepares for death as he prepares for a journey with visa, currency and luggage, and relates by sense that the Hour is nearer to a person than the strap of his sandal. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:24:00}
- He counts obscene speech among the tongue's faults, relates by sense that the Prophet forbade a man to curse his parents and explained it as cursing another's father so that he curses one's own, and tells that Aisha answered someone who greeted ill with a sharp retort and the Prophet told her the believer is neither obscene nor foul. (needs_source: the reports are by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:26:00}
- He likens the believer to a bee that eats what is good, produces what is good and does not break the twig, and says the believer is light and not heavy company. This is his image. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:27:00}
- He speaks of the faults of the eye and the first glance, and tells that a Companion looked at a woman until he struck a wall and his head was cut, and the verse on lowering the gaze was revealed. See [[passage/quran-24-30|al-Nur, verse 30]]. (needs_source: the report has no chain or name) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:29:00}
- He tells that Anas ibn Malik's eye fell on a woman's anklet and he went to Uthman, who said "what is it with one of you who enters with the trace of adultery in his eyes". (needs_source: the report has no chain and its reading is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:29:30}
- He tells that al-Shafi'i covered the left-hand page with his sleeve to memorise, and that once his eye fell on a woman's heel and he took four minutes to memorise what had taken two, and his teacher told him to leave disobedience. (needs_source: the report about al-Shafi'i and his teacher has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:31:00}
- He says lowering the gaze includes lowering it from people's faults and overlooking them, and tells that Shurayh, when a woman made a sound on the dais, asked her to raise her voice so she would think he had not heard. (needs_source: the report of Shurayh has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:32:30}
- He tells that when a man in worn clothes came while the Prophet was on the pulpit, the Prophet told the people to give charity so that his state would be seen. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:33:30}
- He calls for a "moral spring" in which the community honours its elders, shows mercy to the young and knows the due of scholars and of the weak such as the orphan, the widow, the sick and the elderly. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:35:00}
- He tells that Abu Hurayra stood surety for a man who had killed the owner of an orchard and asked to return to his tribe first; the man came back so that it not be said loyalty had gone, and the victim's family pardoned him so that it not be said pardon had gone. (needs_source: the story has no chain and its start is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:38:00}
- He says the community will win only through values and character, criticises bribery and the gifts drug companies give doctors, and wishes every ministry and body had a religious adviser to say what is lawful and forbidden. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep03 00:40:30}

## Episode 4: the wife of Imran, the causes of women's sadness and the breakdown of the family
- He recites the verses on the wife of Imran, who vowed what was in her womb to God, and says these were households tied to God; she hoped for a boy who would serve in al-Aqsa Mosque. He recalls that the land of Sham once breathed spirituality and that it has been turned to something else by our shortcomings and distance from God's words, and says he has dealt with Mary and Jesus in other programmes. See [[passage/quran-3-35|Al Imran, verse 35]], [[passage/quran-3-36|Al Imran, verse 36]] and [[passage/quran-3-37|Al Imran, verse 37]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:03:30}
- He pauses at the words "the male is not like the female" and explains that serving the mosque, with its cleaning, going before dawn and waiting after the night prayer, is work suited to a man and not to a woman. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:04:30}
- He holds that barriers have fallen in some societies where a third sex has appeared, and says a fourth and fifth may appear. This is his opinion and his wording. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:05:00}
- He says the American definition of the family after 1950 was a husband, wife and two children under eighteen living in one home in affection, that about twenty-five years later it became a man and a woman, and that in 2000 it became a group living under one roof, which he calls the definition of misery. (needs_source: the figures and definitions have no source and the reading is garbled in places) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:06:00}
- He tells that a woman sociologist wrote a booklet in 1910 on wedding-night etiquette and a federal court sentenced her to forty-five years in prison, and that a nurse wrote a memo on contraception in California, was sentenced to death and fled to Europe on the night of the execution, and concludes that moral values changed within one century. (needs_source: both stories have no source and the dates are garbled in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:11:00}
- He reports that Arnold Toynbee studied fifty-two kinds of civilisation and found them all gone when their moral side dissolved. (needs_source: the report about Toynbee has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:07:00}
- He gives examples of character such as honouring the elder, the pregnant woman and scholars, quotes a line of Ahmad Shawqi to the sense that nations are their morals and when these go they go, and says character is part of the Prophet's mission, which he calls a quarter of it; then he stops at reciting and explains it as following the Book, not merely reading it. See [[passage/quran-3-164|Al Imran, verse 164]]. (needs_source: the verse is garbled in the transcript and identified from its wording, as is the phrase about a quarter of the mission) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:08:30}
- He criticises those who confine Ramadan to reading the Qur'an and then close the mosque on Eid and leave the copy on the shelf, asks rhetorically how many copies of the Qur'an existed in the Muslim world at a time he dates to 35 AH compared with the number in one house today, and criticises putting a copy under a child's head or banknotes in it for blessing, saying society needs spiritual, psychological and religious treatment. This is his opinion and comparison. (needs_source: the passage is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:10:00}
- He holds that the usual reading of events is flawed and says he wants a sound reading and a sound frame. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:11:30}
- He describes the breakdown of the family: a father asks for five minutes with his children and cannot gather them except by switching off the internet, so they come out of their rooms like insects; he recalls villages without electricity where children went to the mosques to study by lamplight. This is what he observes. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:13:00}
- He criticises using the phone during tawaf, says tawaf is part of prayer because it requires ablution, likens it to phoning one's household while in front of a ruler or minister, and says God's house deserves more courtesy. (needs_source: his statement that tawaf requires ablution has no source, and the rest of the passage is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:14:00}
- He returns to Al Imran to say that priorities in building homes need re-ordering, criticises marriage demands for a five-room house, a car, a gift of jewellery, the dower and the deferred dower, and says some trade in the girl. This is his opinion. (needs_source: part of the passage is a repeated loop) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:16:00}
- He relates by sense that the Prophet told people to marry off one whose religion and character please them, otherwise there will be trial on earth and great corruption, and says Islam attends to material means as it attends to the girl's feelings; he criticises a mother who presses her daughter to accept a man who does not pray. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:17:30}
- He calls on Arab governments to require engaged couples to hold certificates of freedom from physical and psychological illness, as he said in an earlier episode, cites "whoever cheats us is not of us", and says cheating begins in our dealings with one another. (needs_source: the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:18:00}
- He says he has been in preaching for about forty-two or forty-three years and that ninety per cent of the problems brought to him are between husbands and wives, and that women complain of husbands who are foul-mouthed, deceitful, bigoted, rigid and without tenderness. (needs_source: the percentage is his own estimate without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:19:00}
- He advises that God placed a woman's emotion before her reason, says that if a woman's reason were placed before her emotion she would show a man what he has never seen, and so calls for respecting her emotion; he criticises adverts that sell shampoo and the like as a key to personality, and contrasts his grandmother, who lived eighty years with long hair and no shampoo. This is his opinion. (needs_source: part of the passage is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:21:00}
- He says that if a man's emotion came before his reason his wife would be wretched in this world and the next, so his reason should stay first. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:22:00}
- He says this world is a house of trial and not of honour, and that wealth and poverty are both trials, the rich being watched for gratitude and the poor for patience. See [[passage/quran-21-35|al-Anbiya, verse 35]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:23:00}
- He pictures a woman in the kitchen from noon until sunset who is then asked for tea, coffee, sweets and fruit, says customs come before acts of worship with us, and jokes that the man might pray the night prayer and tarawih while his wife washes the dishes and then they swap. This is his description. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:24:00}
- He makes the first cause of women's sadness the loss of awareness of the reality of religion, and says God may give a woman a short-tempered or stingy man in order to raise her rank with Him. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:25:00}
- He makes the second cause women's neglect of supplication and quick weariness of it, relates by sense that God is angry if He is not asked, and compares a father who tires of a son who keeps asking. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:26:00}
- He makes the third cause a woman's disobedience to her parents before marriage, and tells of a seller of clay pots whose son beat him in the street after the pots broke, and the father told the crowd to leave him because he had struck his own father at that very spot thirty-five years before; then he says "as you judge, so you will be judged" and that kindness does not wear out, sin is not forgotten and the Judge does not die. (needs_source: the story and the saying have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:27:30}
- He passes on from scholars that an angry look at one's parents is disobedience. (needs_source: the reading is garbled and the report has no chain; what follows is a repeated loop) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:28:00}
- He makes the fourth cause a mother's grief for a lost child, to the point of keeping his cup and pyjamas, relates by sense that the Prophet wept for his son Ibrahim and said they say only what pleases their Lord, and says grief is natural and a person has a limit to what he can bear. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:30:00}
- He makes the fifth cause lack of contentment and comparing oneself with a sister-in-law or a brother's car, tells of a man told to count his sheep who had only two ewes, and mentions that God burdens no soul beyond its capacity. See [[passage/quran-2-286|al-Baqara, verse 286]]. (needs_source: the tale and the saying attributed to the Prophet are garbled and without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:31:00}
- He makes the sixth cause the craze of advertising and cosmetic clinics with ageing, relates by sense that God is shy of the elderly person, says God created the human being in the best form and that faith makes the face shine, and criticises cosmetic surgery. See [[passage/quran-95-4|al-Tin, verse 4]]. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:34:30}
- He makes the seventh cause a woman's failure in bringing up her children and the husband's absence, and says grandmothers who made their lives a project of upbringing produced scholars, writers and doctors. (needs_source: the claim has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:35:30}
- He makes the eighth cause the need some women have to work, calls the working woman "the superwoman", holds that a woman with no need to work daily should not go out, cites God's words to women to stay in their homes, and counts the cost of going out in clothes, transport and twenty bosses in place of one at home. This is his opinion and not the museum's judgement. See [[passage/quran-33-33|al-Ahzab, verse 33]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:37:00}
- He makes the ninth cause being forced to marry a cousin. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:37:00}
- He makes the tenth cause a woman's dislike of her husband marrying again, says the law permits plural marriage and obliges justice, that many who want a second wife are not just to the first, that the media have turned women against the matter, and that the word betrayal is a media term and not a religious one. This is his opinion on a disputed matter. (needs_source: part of the passage is a repeated loop) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:38:30}
- He makes the eleventh cause a husband's foul tongue, and says listening is an art few master, citing a saying to the sense that some speech is arts. (needs_source: the saying has no attribution) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:39:00}
- He makes the twelfth cause the hostility of the husband's family, describes a mother-in-law who meddles in everything, advises her to be light-hearted and loved and not to spoil things for her son and his wife, and advises the wife to treat her husband's mother well and encourage him to be dutiful to her. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:40:00}
- He makes the thirteenth cause wives' passion for shopping, says a survey found ninety-eight per cent of women love it, and describes a man waiting two or three hours. (needs_source: the survey has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:41:00}
- He closes with an Arabic saying to the sense that you are quick to take offence and I am quick to err so we will not quarrel, and hopes for that between spouses. (needs_source: the saying has no attribution and the reading is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep04 00:41:30}

## Episode 5: do not despair of God's mercy, sincerity in deeds and the rights of people
- He recalls the programme's method of pausing at one or two verses to draw a lesson and not to interpret, and of walking in the procession of light as the early generations did, away from groups that lead people from the middle way of the religion; he then says the verse leaves joy in every heart because we are all sinners. See [[passage/quran-39-53|al-Zumar, verse 53]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:02:30}
- He speaks of the arrogant person who is proud even toward God because he sees himself as protected and the best in faith, and says the door of good is not closed to anyone. (needs_source: the sentence breaks off in a garbled repetition at 3:30) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:03:00}
- He holds that opening a new page with God needs a deed that is sincere and correct, passes on Umar's prayer "I ask You for the deed that is purest and most correct", and explains that purest means for God, not so that people say he prays, gives or does good. (needs_source: the saying of Umar has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:05:00}
- He relates by sense that the first three through whom the fire is kindled on the Day of Resurrection are a scholar, a generous man and a martyr: each is made to know his blessing, is asked what he did with it and says he taught, spent or fought, and is told he did it so that it be said scholar, generous or brave, and it was said, and is dragged on his face into the fire; he gives as examples one who teaches to be famous and one who advertises his donations in the newspapers. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:08:00}
- He says some people unknowingly set up partners with God when they thank the doctor alone for saving a patient and do not mention God's grace and mercy, and holds that God is the healer who puts the doctor to work. This is his opinion. (needs_source: the word before "with God" is garbled in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:08:30}
- He advises anyone whose relative is ill or facing an operation to make three prayers: that God grant the doctor understanding to diagnose the illness as it is, inspire him to prescribe the right medicine, and place the secret of healing in the medicine, since many patients take the same medicine from the same doctor and some are healed and others not; he criticises seeking connections to reach a famous doctor. This is his opinion and advice. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:10:30}
- He tells that Moses asked his Lord whether healing is not from Him, God said yes, and Moses asked what the doctors do, and God said they comfort souls until my healing or my decree comes. (needs_source: the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:09:30}
- He counts ruqya, which is reciting the Fatiha, laying the hand on the place of pain, saying the name of God three times and "I seek refuge in God's might and power from the evil of what I find and fear" seven times, as a medical prescription from the Prophet, mentions the prayer "O God, heal, there is no healing but Your healing", says supplication is not answered for one who eats the forbidden, and relates by sense that the Prophet told Sa'd to make his food good so that his prayer be answered. (needs_source: the reports and prayers are without a chain or by sense) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:12:30}
- He calls for deeds to be correct, that is according to the Sunna, criticises sending phone messages with a fabricated hadith and asking people to forward them for reward, and says one should not heed them without verifying. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:13:00}
- He draws four points from the verse: that God's mercy encompasses everything, that human nature inclines to disobedience, that only God forgives sins, and that the servant comes to God with the humility of a sinner, praying as a beggar seeking refuge and not as one who demands, likening it to a poor man who asks and a son who asks his father. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:14:30}
- He recommends opening a prayer of supplication with praise of God, then asking forgiveness and repenting, saying "there is no god but God" and glorifying Him, then blessing the Prophet, then asking what one wishes and closing with blessings on the Prophet again. (needs_source: the order has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:15:00}
- He criticises reading written supplications from a booklet for each circuit around the Kaaba and says he wants supplication that comes from the heart. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:15:30}
- He tells of a man on a ship in a storm whose companions asked him to pray, and he prostrated and said "Lord, You showed us Your power, show us Your mercy", and the sea calmed; he applies this to wronged peoples and those living in tents after having been honoured, and prays that God guide the wrongdoer or take him. (needs_source: the story has no chain and the name and the last phrase are garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:16:30}
- He says that anyone who wants to cut off a man's finger should leave him to God, who will cut off his arm for him, and anyone who wants to cut off his arm should leave him to God, who will cut his news from the whole world; he says he has said this before. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:17:00}
- He urges standing in the last third of the night instead of sitting before serials, relates by sense that God descends to the lowest heaven then and calls for whoever seeks forgiveness, repents or has a need, and adds that God stretches out His hand at night for the sinner of the day to repent and by day for the sinner of the night. (needs_source: the reports are by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:18:00}
- He says the doors of this world close and God's door does not, that when a servant says "Lord" God answers "at Your service, My servant" even if he is disobedient, relates by sense that God rejoices in His servant's repentance, likens God to a doctor who hates the illness and not the patient, and then relates the divine hadith on drawing near by a span, then an arm's length, then a fathom, on walking and running, and on remembering God in an assembly. (needs_source: the reports are by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:19:30}
- He explains "My servants" by saying there are two kinds of servant, a servant of worship and a servant of enslavement, and that one who serves money all his life is wretched, and relates by sense the Prophet's supplication of misery against the slave of the dinar and the dirham. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:21:00}
- He says wealth is never mentioned without evil and that its name comes from inclination because it inclines people away from the truth. This is his view on language. (needs_source: the derivation has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:21:00}
- He speaks of bribery and officials' gifts, relates by sense that gifts to officials are misappropriation, and tells that a man brought Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz a gift and he refused, the man said the Prophet accepted gifts, and Umar replied that for the Prophet it was a gift and for us a bribe. (needs_source: the reports have no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:22:30}
- He relates by sense that the briber, the one bribed and the go-between are in the fire, and says the rule of necessities applies only to great calamities and not to ordinary matters. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain, and the application of necessity is his own) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:23:00}
- He calls on the employee to know that his provision is in heaven and not in the treasury or the bank. See [[passage/quran-51-22|al-Dhariyat, verse 22]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:23:30}
- He explains that repentance means stopping, regret, resolve not to return and giving back rights, and that debts are of two kinds: debts to the Creator and debts to creatures. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:24:00}
- He suggests that to make up missed prayers one prays with each current time one or two of what is owed, or wakes before dawn and makes up prayers, and relates by sense that deeds are by intentions so that if one intends this and dies it is written as if he had prayed all that was owed. (needs_source: the suggestion and the report have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:25:30}
- He takes up the make-up of fasting for a woman who did not fast through ignorance caused by wrong jurisprudence, says postnatal bleeding lasts at most forty days and that if it stops sooner she prays and fasts, and that she counts the Ramadans missed in pregnancy and nursing. (needs_source: his statements on jurisprudence have no source and the reading is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:26:30}
- He counts among debts an oath not expiated and a vow not fulfilled, and says the expiation of an oath is feeding ten poor people or fasting three days. (needs_source: his statement on expiation has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:26:30}
- He counts among debts an employee's use of the institution's car, computer, papers and phone for his own affairs, and says he must return it to the institution or give it a useful gift if it is private, and then that the rights of people are forgiven only when the servant gets their pardon and returns their rights. (needs_source: part of the passage is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:28:30}
- He tells that Sahl ibn Abdullah set out on a journey of learning and robbers cut the road and took the caravan's goods, and he saw their leader not eating from the loot and saying he was fasting that Thursday and had left a door between himself and God that he might enter one day; Sahl later saw him circling the Kaaba and weeping. (needs_source: the story has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:30:00}
- He tells that Moses prayed for rain for his people and God said He would not send water until the sinner left them, Moses called and no one came out, then rain fell, and Moses said no sinner left, and God said a sinner had repented between Him and him, and Moses asked to be shown him in order to learn repentance, and God said I covered him when he sinned, shall I expose him now he has repented. (needs_source: the story has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:32:00}
- He quotes one of the righteous as saying that he fears stones will fall for the number of sins rather than waiting for rain. (needs_source: the saying is unattributed) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:31:00}
- He tells of a wayward young man whom his friends embraced and he repented, and then they asked him to tell his story in the mosque and he was embarrassed; he says people should not expose one another and one should overlook others' faults, as he said in an earlier episode. (needs_source: the story is garbled and without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:33:00}
- He says God attributed the servants to Himself in honour of them, likens the criminal-record certificate institutions ask for to the record of our deeds, says it would be full of offences if the angel of evil deeds produced it, and yet God does not remove the servant from the rank of servanthood. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:35:00}
- He explains forgiveness as wiping and covering, likens it to a wind that effaces a trace, and says the doors of earth close and the doors of heaven do not. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:35:30}
- He explains the verse's ending with the Forgiving, the Merciful: a father forgives his son two or three times and then reproaches him, but God does not reproach a servant who comes to Him nor remind him of past sins; he cites God's call to turn to Him and submit before the punishment comes. See [[passage/quran-39-54|al-Zumar, verse 54]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:37:30}
- He calls for repentance from idling the limbs from what they were made for and using them for disobedience, gives the example of the eye made to look at God's creation and show mercy to orphans being used to look at the forbidden, calls this a compound calamity, and says blessings call for thanking the Giver. (needs_source: part of the passage is a repeated loop) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:39:00}
- He urges seizing the gifts of the month by returning to God and seeking forgiveness for every sin, small and great. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep05 00:39:00}

## Episode 6: if we had listened or reasoned, and the sound heart
- He pauses at the words of the people of the blaze that had they listened or reasoned they would not be among them, and says they had ears and minds but did not benefit from them, and that the loss of hearing and reason is the cause of their end. See [[passage/quran-67-10|al-Mulk, verse 10]] and [[passage/quran-67-11|al-Mulk, verse 11]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:02:00}
- He holds that any calamity not in religion is not a calamity, since wealth, health and a house can be made up, whereas the true calamity is to be struck in one's religion, as when a man's lawful money becomes forbidden. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:03:00}
- He notes that the Qur'an guides to what is most upright, then says the first thing he wants to treat is people's plunging into one another's honour. See [[passage/quran-17-9|al-Isra, verse 9]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:03:30}
- He advises hearing from a person and not about him before judging him, because the one who passes things on may dislike him or interpret his words as he wishes, producing misunderstanding. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:04:30}
- He says listening is an art, that God gave a person two ears and one mouth, that the wise listens four times as much as he speaks and the ignorant speaks seven times as much as he hears, and says an Arab speaks about 7,500 words a day and an Arab woman about 12,500. (needs_source: the ratios and statistics have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:05:30}
- He likens the silent person to the fish that keeps its mouth shut and is not caught, says a person regrets a word he said and not one he left unsaid, and that erring twenty times in pardon is better than erring once in punishment. (needs_source: the sayings are unattributed) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:06:30}
- He says God made the Fire in levels and Paradise in degrees, with eight open gates in Paradise including the gate of al-Rayyan and the gates of fasting, the patient, those who strive and knowledge, whereas the gates of the Fire are shut with extended columns so that none escapes. See [[passage/quran-104-8|al-Humaza, verse 8]] and [[passage/quran-104-9|al-Humaza, verse 9]]. (needs_source: the names of the gates have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:07:30}
- He contrasts Western schools, without walls and which pupils are glad to go to, with our schools, which our children regard as prisons with a scowling teacher, a heavy curriculum and pressure to succeed, then says the gates of Paradise are open. This is his opinion and comparison. (needs_source: his description of Western schools has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:08:30}
- He says God gave a person two roads, the road of safety and the road of regret, each with its steps. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:09:00}
- He makes the first step on the road of the righteous hearing God's Book as though it came down for him alone, and says the hard heart is one that does not weep at the verses of Paradise or stir at scenes of refugees in shelters, and one that carries unmade prayers and fasts and does not busy itself with asking forgiveness. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-39-22|al-Zumar, verse 22]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:10:30}
- He relates by sense that whoever does not care for the affairs of the Muslims is not of them. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:10:00}
- He divides hearts into sound, sick and dead, describes the sound as free of deceit, rancour, malice and envy, and ties it to the one who comes to God with a sound heart. See [[passage/quran-26-88|al-Shu'ara, verse 88]] and [[passage/quran-26-89|al-Shu'ara, verse 89]]. (needs_source: he attributes the threefold division to scholars without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:11:30}
- He mentions the removal of rancour from the breasts of the people of Paradise and the prayer that God put no rancour in hearts toward those who believe. See [[passage/quran-15-47|al-Hijr, verse 47]] and [[passage/quran-59-10|al-Hashr, verse 10]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:12:00}
- He urges giving up envy and says God distributed livelihood to try the rich through the poor and the poor through the rich, and faults a scholar who is content to write in his cell and does not go down to the people. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-43-32|al-Zukhruf, verse 32]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:13:00}
- He says the mosque has seven benefits, among them brotherhood in God, and relates by sense that whoever purifies at home and goes to the mosque, God receives him and welcomes him as a household welcomes a returning absentee, and that were God to punish the people of the earth the people of the mosques would be saved. (needs_source: the rest of the list is garbled and the reports are by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:14:00}
- He criticises those who fill the mosques in Ramadan and then abandon prayer and fasting, and calls for worshipping the Lord of Ramadan and not Ramadan, with continuous worship and not seasonal. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:14:30}
- He says the caller who says "come to prayer" calls everyone who hears the call, and tells that Umar missed a Companion at the dawn prayer and went to him and said that Umar calls you and you answer, and God calls you and you do not. (needs_source: the story has no chain and the Companion is not named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:15:30}
- He says some people are carried to the mosque by their sons while ill so as to pray in congregation, passes on from scholars that the believer in the mosque is like a fish in water and the hypocrite like a bird in a cage, criticises one who looks at his watch during tarawih, and calls for accustoming children to the mosque from childhood. (needs_source: the saying attributed to scholars has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:16:30}
- He relates by sense that if people relied on God as He should be relied on He would provide for them as for the birds that go out hungry and return full, and says the prophets met trials while their hearts were sound. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:17:30}
- He mentions that Adam, the first human, was deceived by Iblis, who told them they would be angels or immortals and swore to them, and that Adam told his son he had not thought anyone would swear falsely by God. See [[passage/quran-7-20|al-A'raf, verse 20]] and [[passage/quran-7-21|al-A'raf, verse 21]]. (needs_source: Adam's words have no chain and the name of the narrator is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:21:00}
- He says people still seek immortality and cites those who go to doctors because they want a child. This is his opinion. (needs_source: part of the passage is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:22:00}
- He says that when Satan tells him to go easy on himself he keeps within the limits of the law: he lowers his gaze, takes no one's money and does not backbite; and holds that a person's real age is measured by faith and deeds and not by the number of years. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:19:00}
- He passes on from scholars that whoever misses a gathering of knowledge for three days has a sick heart, and tells that Umar would take Abu Musa al-Ash'ari by the hand and say come, let us believe for an hour. (needs_source: both reports have no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:20:00}
- He tells that Abraham sent away an unbelieving guest and God reproached him that He had provided for the man for fifty years, so Abraham ran after him and the man accepted Islam; he then says find seventy excuses for your brother. (needs_source: the story and the saying have no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep06 00:23:30}
- He quotes two verses to the sense that one who laughs before you may carry a burden, and says people are loads of trials and one should look for excuses for them.
