- He says an illness that has a cure is not a real illness and the real illness is of the heart, hatred, rancour, pride, envy and showing off, and relates by sense that showing off runs in the son of Adam like blood and that among the seven shaded by God is a man who gave charity so secretly that his left hand does not know what his right gave. (needs_source: both reports are by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:12:00}
- He says if a thief steals something, one should thank God that faith was not stolen from the heart. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:12:30}
- He explains the verse as meaning that God knows the Prophet's chest tightens because the idolaters deny divinity, the message and the Qur'an and were amazed at making the gods one God, and tells that an old friend in the 1970s asked an idol worshipper in a foreign land how many gods he had and he answered that every matter has a god. See [[passage/quran-38-5|Sad, verse 5]]. (needs_source: the tale has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:13:30}
- He mentions that the unbelievers called the Prophet a sorcerer, a soothsayer, a poet and a madman, and that God answered for him and he did not answer, and cites God's denial. See [[passage/quran-15-6|al-Hijr, verse 6]], [[passage/quran-69-41|al-Haqqa, verse 41]], [[passage/quran-69-42|al-Haqqa, verse 42]] and [[passage/quran-68-2|al-Qalam, verse 2]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:15:00}
- He says the cure for tightness from people's words is glorifying God, relates by sense that "glory to God and praise" are two words light on the tongue, heavy in the scale and dear to the Merciful, that a palm is planted in Paradise for whoever says them, and likens continued good deeds to building a palace that goes up only as one pays for it. (needs_source: the reports are by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:16:30}
- He relates by sense that the Prophet said guard yourselves from the Fire even with half a date, mentions the report of the woman who entered the Fire for a cat she shut up, and says one who imprisons a people or a nation is a greater sinner. (needs_source: the reports are by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:17:00}
- He says we do not defend Islam but present it, that Islam is not accused and the accused is the one not convinced by it, who should be put on the defensive. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:18:00}
- He says trial can come by giving, such as office, wealth and children, and mentions Qarun and the keys of his treasuries, which a band of strong men could scarcely carry, and that when the earth swallowed him those who had wished for his like said what they said. See [[passage/quran-28-76|al-Qasas, verse 76]], [[passage/quran-28-79|al-Qasas, verse 79]] and [[passage/quran-28-81|al-Qasas, verse 81]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:19:00}
- He says you do not know what the rich man has of psychological illness, strain with his children and need of medicines to sleep and eat, that the believer is optimistic and the non-believer pessimistic, and mentions a saying about selling shrouds and henna. This is his opinion. (needs_source: the saying has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:21:00}
- He says whoever is stoned with people's talk should build from the stones a staircase to climb, and that a road that is perfectly smooth may mean a mistake. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:22:00}
- He relates by sense that when God created Paradise He told Gabriel to look at it, and Gabriel said by Your might none will hear of it and not enter, so He surrounded it with hardships; and when He created the Fire He told him to look and he said none will hear of it and enter, so He surrounded it with desires. He lists the hardships as patience with the harm of spouse, neighbour and ruler, and the desires as backbiting, tale-bearing, serials, films, cafes, drugs and wine. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain, and the lists are his) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:24:30}
- He says the believer sees things as they are and the non-believer sees them upside down, so opening for the believer is memorising the Qur'an, fasting Mondays and Thursdays and giving charity and not money. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:25:30}
- He mentions that God told the Prophet he might destroy himself with grief over his people if they do not believe, that what is on the earth is an adornment to test them, and that He will make it barren dust. See [[passage/quran-18-6|al-Kahf, verse 6]], [[passage/quran-18-7|al-Kahf, verse 7]] and [[passage/quran-18-8|al-Kahf, verse 8]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:26:30}
- He tells that Aisha saw the Prophet on a winter night rise, make ablution and weep, and he told her how can I take ease in sleep when the one with the horn has put it to his mouth and bent his forehead waiting to be ordered to blow. (needs_source: the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:28:30}
- He tells that after the first blast God asks the angel of death who remains and he says Gabriel, Israfil and Michael, and God orders him to take their souls and then the angel of death dies; then God says to whom is the kingdom today and answers to God, the One, the Prevailing; then the second blast comes and they stand up looking, and the believers say this is what the Merciful promised. See [[passage/quran-39-68|al-Zumar, verse 68]], [[passage/quran-40-16|Ghafir, verse 16]], [[passage/quran-36-51|Ya Sin, verse 51]] and [[passage/quran-36-52|Ya Sin, verse 52]]. (needs_source: the account is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:32:00}
- He says whoever's power tempts him to wrong people should remember God's power over him, relates by sense that God will take retribution for the hornless sheep from the horned one and that people will return needle and thread on the Day of Resurrection, and criticises those who treat public funds and banks as lawful to take. See [[passage/quran-21-47|al-Anbiya, verse 47]]. (needs_source: the reports are by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:34:00}
- He says a person's age is measured by deeds and not days, and tells that Umar used to ask anyone whose appearance pleased him what he had given to Islam. (needs_source: the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:35:30}
- He recommends keeping company with scholars and the righteous and says the dog that accompanied the People of the Cave is mentioned in the Qur'an, and that Abu Talib did not keep to the company of the people of truth to the end: when death came the Prophet asked him to say the declaration of oneness and he said he was on the religion of Abd al-Muttalib, with Abu Jahl and Abu Lahab seated beside him. See [[passage/quran-18-18|al-Kahf, verse 18]] and [[passage/quran-28-56|al-Qasas, verse 56]]. (needs_source: the account of Abu Talib's death has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep12 00:38:00}

## Episode 13: Pharaoh's exaltation and humility
- He pauses at the opening of Surat al-Qasas on Pharaoh exalting himself in the land, splitting its people into factions and weakening one group, and on God's will to favour the weakened and make them leaders, and says the verses draw a frame for the weak believers and warn those who thought they own the earth. See [[passage/quran-28-1|al-Qasas, verse 1]], [[passage/quran-28-2|al-Qasas, verse 2]], [[passage/quran-28-3|al-Qasas, verse 3]], [[passage/quran-28-4|al-Qasas, verse 4]], [[passage/quran-28-5|al-Qasas, verse 5]] and [[passage/quran-28-6|al-Qasas, verse 6]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:02:30}
- He says the earth is God's and He gives it to whom He wills and that the abode of the hereafter is for those who want neither exaltation on earth nor corruption, and distinguishes positive exaltation, which is exaltation by humility, from negative exaltation. See [[passage/quran-7-128|al-A'raf, verse 128]] and [[passage/quran-28-83|al-Qasas, verse 83]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:03:30}
- He recalls the Prophet's humility among his Companions, sitting where the gathering ended and a Bedouin coming in asking which of you is the son of Abd al-Muttalib, and tells that a Bedouin trembled in awe and the Prophet put his hand on his shoulder and said take it easy, I am only the son of a woman who ate dried meat in Mecca. (needs_source: the reports are given by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:04:30}
- He tells that Abu Bakr walked beside Usama, who was riding, as he saw off his army, Abu Bakr being over sixty, and refused to ride or let Usama dismount, saying what harm if my feet are dusty for an hour in God's way. (needs_source: the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:05:30}
- He says every blessing is envied except humility, that people claim humility but do not like to be humble, and mentions that Abu Sufyan liked boasting and the Prophet told him whoever enters Abu Sufyan's house is safe, and says people have an Abu Sufyan gene, loving to be praised for what they did not do. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-3-188|Al Imran, verse 188]]. (needs_source: the Prophet's words to Abu Sufyan are by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:07:00}
- He relates by sense that when the world turns to a person it lends him the good qualities of others and when it turns away it strips his own, and prays that the world be in our hands and not in our hearts. (needs_source: the saying is unattributed) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:08:00}
- He relates by sense the prohibition on wishing for death because of harm, and the prayer let him say O God keep me alive so long as life is better for me, says no one knows whether the dead have found rest, and advises one who carries the world on his head that the solver of knots is God alone and not a thousand solvers. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:09:30}
- He says negative exaltation is never praised in the Qur'an, that Pharaoh exalted himself in the earth and not in heaven, that the ground we tread may cover the graves of our forebears, and that cities move cemeteries to cut roads, so a proud man may tread on his grandfather's remains. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:11:30}
- He tells that Abd al-Malik ibn Marwan stood before the mirror at twenty-eight and said I am the young king if only it lasted, and his guard said if it had lasted for those before you it would not have reached you, and if it lasts for you it will not reach those after you. (needs_source: the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:13:00}
- He mentions God's words that the Children of Israel would spread corruption twice and rise greatly, and Solomon's words do not exalt yourselves against me and come to me in submission, and says a human being is made of dust and should not look down on God's creatures. See [[passage/quran-17-4|al-Isra, verse 4]] and [[passage/quran-27-31|al-Naml, verse 31]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:16:00}
- He quotes lines to the sense that a human is from earth and returns to it so he should not boast of his body or money, and says people are made to serve one another. (needs_source: the lines are unattributed) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:16:00}
- He explains Pharaoh's making the people factions by dividing them into classes, as in communist thought and in the Roman system, and into a half-god, and says the ruler's thinking is that whoever is with me is a saint and whoever is against me is Iblis. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:17:30}
- He passes on from Abu Hanifa that our view is right with the possibility of error and others' view is wrong with the possibility of being right, and says a human being is not the centre of the universe. (needs_source: the saying ascribed to Abu Hanifa has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:18:30}
- He relates that if Iblis wins three things from the son of Adam, he is in his hand like a ball: self-admiration, thinking his deeds many, and forgetting his sins; he likens the self-admiring to someone in a room of mirrors. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:23:30}
- He says luxury is dangerous, contrasts his grandmother washing by hand with the washing machine, and cites a saying he ascribes to Kennedy that six of seven young Americans are unfit for military service. This is his opinion. (needs_source: the ascription to Kennedy has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:26:00}
- He tells that a boy of thirteen wanted to join a campaign and stood on tiptoe to look tall so that the Prophet would accept him. (needs_source: the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:26:30}
- He says scholars hold two views on forgetting a sin after repentance: one says do not forget it, so that one keeps asking forgiveness and is not vain about one's obedience, and the other says forget it; and he tells that a scholar was asked whether to remember his sin and said that in sin I was distant from God and in repentance I am in purity. He gives both views out of scholarly trust. (needs_source: the ascription of both views and the saying to scholars has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:30:30}
- He says whoever regrets a sin even for a blink has it removed, that some scholars count regret as a reward, and that good should take the place of the sin as one builds up good in place of what he tore down by backbiting. (needs_source: the saying is unattributed) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:31:30}
- He recalls that a believer's honour is greater with God than the sanctity of the Kaaba, likens backbiting to eating a dead brother's flesh, and says backbiting has a form of Pharaonic pride. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-49-12|al-Hujurat, verse 12]]. (needs_source: his words on the believer's sanctity are by sense) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:33:00}
- He says some people take customs for religion, such as how to eat, dress and ride a donkey, that the true Sunna is humility, affection, forbearance and patience, and criticises one who enters Islam and at once lets his beard grow, frowns and avoids laughing. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:35:00}
- He mentions the words "and your clothes purify" and says each people have their dress and the law requires covering the private parts; he says some jurists held that the whole face should be covered and the majority of jurists hold that it is a virtue and not an obligation and that a woman may show her face and hands, and criticises a woman who puts on cover for prayer and then takes it off. This is his opinion on a disputed matter. See [[passage/quran-74-4|al-Muddaththir, verse 4]]. (needs_source: the ascription of the two views to jurists has no source and the passage is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:38:00}
- He says he wishes the media would teach religion from its alphabet and its great truths and not trivia. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:38:30}
- He tells the story of the father who brought his sons a bundle of sticks and told them to break it, and they could not, and said if you are together none will break you, then the tale of the white bull in Kalila wa Dimna in which the lion ate the bulls one after another, and says they show unity and not isolation, and links them to the community's neglect of Palestine and then Iraq and Syria. This is his opinion. (needs_source: both stories are told without attribution and his link to the present is his own) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:41:30}
- He says falsehood has its round and the state of falsehood is for an hour and the state of truth until the Hour. (needs_source: the saying is unattributed) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep13 00:42:00}

## Episode 14: the support of the messengers in this life and on the Day the witnesses stand
- He pauses at the verse of Surat Ghafir on God supporting His messengers and the believers in the life of this world and on the Day the witnesses stand, when the excuse of the wrongdoers will not help them and theirs is the curse and the evil abode. See [[passage/quran-40-51|Ghafir, verse 51]] and [[passage/quran-40-52|Ghafir, verse 52]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:00:30}
- He says a civilisation falls when its tie to its culture weakens, that the Umayyad, Abbasid and Ottoman states fell when their tie to the system of the Book and the Sunna was cut, and distinguishes a society that counts crime as abnormal from one that hates virtue and counts it abnormal. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:03:00}
- He mentions that Surat Ghafir is also called the Believer and is built on the idea of the one who speaks the truth, shown in a believing man of Pharaoh's household who hid his faith. See [[passage/quran-40-28|Ghafir, verse 28]] and [[passage/quran-40-29|Ghafir, verse 29]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:02:00}
- He says the messengers faced societies that found divine unity strange and treated the truthful man as abnormal, and that the idolaters inverted the rule of comparison by saying sale is like usury. See [[passage/quran-38-5|Sad, verse 5]] and [[passage/quran-2-275|al-Baqara, verse 275]]. (needs_source: his account of the inverted comparison is his own reasoning) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:05:00}
- He says the Qur'an celebrated the birth of Jesus and of Moses and did not mention the birth of Muhammad but the birth of his message, because the messages of Moses and Jesus rested on their persons and their signs ended when they left, whereas the Prophet's chief miracle is an enduring Book. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:07:30}
- He regrets that many Muslims take a defensive stance for Islam and says Islam is strong in itself and has no connection to its followers, for if it followed them it would long since have vanished. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:08:30}
- He relates by sense that you are all from Adam and Adam is from dust, and explains the word Adam linguistically as from the relish that joins bread to the mouth, so that people are joined to one another. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain, and the derivation is his own) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:10:30}
- He says God supported the messengers with conclusive proofs, which are authority, and reviews the stories of Noah after nine hundred and fifty years, Hud, Salih and his she-camel and its hamstringing, Shu'ayb, and Abraham and his people's fire. See [[passage/quran-54-10|al-Qamar, verse 10]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:13:00}
- He tells that Joseph's brothers threw him in the well and later took his brother Benjamin, says Joseph's vindication came after about forty years, and that a Muslim is hostile only to Satan and those on his road and not to relatives, neighbours and employers. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-12-8|Yusuf, verse 8]], [[passage/quran-12-9|Yusuf, verse 9]], [[passage/quran-12-80|Yusuf, verse 80]] and [[passage/quran-12-90|Yusuf, verse 90]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:15:30}
- He says Joseph's brothers thought that killing or banishing him would clear their father's face for them and make them a righteous people, and says he once thought that building righteousness on killing was a trait of the Children of Israel only and then saw that many who claim Islam build their edifices on the like. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:22:30}
- He says offices are not inherited, and tells that Umar said after being stabbed that it is enough for the house of al-Khattab that one of them be called to account for the community of Muhammad, that Abu Dharr said you are weak and it is a trust and on the Day of Resurrection disgrace and regret, and that when Umar asked Abu Hurayra to return as governor of Bahrain he declined, saying Joseph was a prophet son of a prophet. (needs_source: the reports have no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:19:00}
- He describes what happened to Joseph in the well, saying God inspired him that he would tell them of their deed while they did not perceive it, and tells whoever wants the world to run his way that if it did the heavens and earth would be corrupted, giving the examples of Abraham, Ishmael and the migration. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:21:00}
- He explains that killing one soul unjustly is as if one killed all people because it kills humanity, and that whoever gives life to himself by piety, remembrance and repentance gives life to all people. This is his explanation. See [[passage/quran-5-32|al-Ma'ida, verse 32]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:23:00}
- He tells that Abu Hanifa leaned on the wall of his Jewish neighbour and asked his pardon after the dawn prayer, and that the neighbour wanted to sell his house for two hundred dinars, a hundred for the house and a hundred for the neighbourhood of Abu Hanifa, and says the neighbour comes before the house. (needs_source: the story has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:24:00}
- He tells that Moses asked God to stop creation, and God said He creates and provides for them and they curse Him. (needs_source: the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:24:30}
- He says the messengers were also supported by their strong character, likens humility to a full ear of wheat that bows against an empty ear that stands high, relates by sense that whoever is humble for God is raised, and quotes lines that a star appears in the water though it is high and smoke rises though it is low. (needs_source: the report is by sense and the lines are unattributed) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:26:00}
- He says God divided livelihoods among people, that one who tries to answer every charge spends his life answering, and that if every barking dog were stoned the rocks would weigh a dinar each. See [[passage/quran-43-32|al-Zukhruf, verse 32]]. (needs_source: the last saying is unattributed) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:27:00}
- He says believers are few, that the Qur'an describes most people as not thankful, not knowing and wicked, and advises walking the road even if its walkers are few. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:28:30}
- He says support in the life of this world may not be seen by people but the believer feels it through his certainty, that the witnesses are the messengers, the angels and the community, and that the middle community bears witness over the nations. See [[passage/quran-2-143|al-Baqara, verse 143]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:30:30}
- He says a servant is rewarded for every affliction except envy, and that the elite said of the believers that they are a small band who enrage us. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-26-54|al-Shu'ara, verse 54]] and [[passage/quran-26-55|al-Shu'ara, verse 55]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:31:30}
- He explains that the wrongdoer is one who has transgressed, that wrong is putting a thing in the wrong place and that their excuse will not help them, explains "theirs is the curse" as their owning it as believers own the good news, and says a believer curses nothing, relating by sense that a curse returns on the one who cursed if its object does not deserve it. This is his explanation. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:34:30}
- He says the evil abode is a severe punishment, that the wrongdoers gave rest to no one in this world, that part of the believers' support is to keep them firm on the truth so that they do not become yes-men, and likens it to the people of the ship. (needs_source: part of the passage is a repeated loop) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:36:00}
- He advises lighting a candle rather than cursing the dark and saying a good word, says negativity breeds zeros, and recites lines of al-Shafi'i on honour. (needs_source: the ascription of the lines to al-Shafi'i has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep14 00:38:30}

## Episode 15: the people of the blaze and five kinds of people from whom blessing is lifted
- He says the Qur'an brings the hereafter before us as if seen with the eye, and pauses at the admission of the people of the fire that they neither listened nor reasoned. He explains that the hearing meant is the hearing of meaning and not only the physical sense, and that they mocked the people of the straight path, walked a road that ends in the abyss and dug their own ends. See [[passage/quran-67-10|al-Mulk, verse 10]] and [[passage/quran-67-11|al-Mulk, verse 11]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:03:00}
- He says the matter is the use of reason, tells of a boy at a small shop in Egypt who would say to the customer take an idea and come back tomorrow, regards it as marketing genius, and understands from it that a person should give himself room to think before deciding. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:04:00}
- He says God points only to good, and that the Prophet left no good unexplained and commanded, and no evil unexplained and warned against it, so the two roads are clear. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:04:30}
- He counts five kinds of people from whom blessing is lifted: the covenant-breaker, the transgressor, the envious, the spiteful and the traitor, and says he reviews himself against them so as not to fall under the sense of the verse about bidding others to righteousness while forgetting oneself. See [[passage/quran-2-44|al-Baqara, verse 44]]. (needs_source: the list of five kinds is given with no attribution) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:05:30}
- He explains the first kind, the one who breaks a [[concept/ahd|covenant]], as someone who pledged himself to a permitted matter or to hearing and obeying and then broke it, holds that the worst breaker of covenants is one who does not uphold God's law in small matters and great, and says he wants first to apply the law to himself and be a Muslim walking on two feet in line with the Qur'an in desire, conduct, speech and action. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:07:00}
- He says a person should be clean outwardly and inwardly and that the outward shows the inward, so that one whose speech is good and whose looking and listening are gentle shows the marks of God's favour, as if people saw his inner self in the beauty of his outer. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:07:30}
- He says the roots of evil are three according to the scholars: [[concept/hasad|envy]], greed and love of the world. He gives the example of a man greedy for the wealth his father left who gives his sisters nothing, and of families that give the males and deny the females, and relates by sense that whoever bars an heir from an inheritance, God bars him from his inheritance in Paradise. See [[passage/quran-23-10|al-Mu'minun, verse 10]] and [[passage/quran-23-11|al-Mu'minun, verse 11]] on the inheritors. The subject is connected to [[concept/mirath|inheritance]]. (needs_source: the division is credited to unnamed scholars, and the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:09:30}
- He presents the second kind, the transgressor, saying a person's transgression returns upon him, likens it to a ball struck against a wall that comes back to the face, cites the proverb that Baraqish sinned against herself, and urges whoever takes people's money, touches their honour or sheds their blood to fear God and to know that erring in [[concept/afw|pardon]] twenty times is better than erring once in punishment, and says stupidity is one of God's soldiers. See [[passage/quran-10-23|Yunus, verse 23]]. (needs_source: the saying on pardon and the saying that stupidity is a soldier of God are given without attribution) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:11:30}
- He presents the third kind, the envious, and mentions the command to seek refuge from his harm in Surat al-Falaq. He says a person should not spend his life imagining he is envied, and recalls what he said before: that one who fears his own eye has struck another takes a trace of that person's water or cup, then performs ablution with it or has it sprinkled so that the effect of envy departs, God willing. See [[passage/quran-113-1|al-Falaq, verse 1]] and [[passage/quran-113-5|al-Falaq, verse 5]]. (needs_source: the remedy is given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:13:00}
- He presents the fourth kind, the spiteful, likening him to a camel that stores up and stores up and then breaks loose all at once. He says spite lifts blessing from the heart, the life, the work, the money, the children, people's affection and sound decisions, and that the spiteful objects to what God gave others of His bounty when God alone is the giver. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:14:00}
- He presents the fifth kind, the traitor, saying he is one into whose heart [[concept/amana|trust]] has found no road. He recalls the trust offered to the heavens, the earth and the mountains and borne by the human being, says the traitor has betrayed the trust of God, His Messenger and the believers, and that God knows the [[concept/khiyana|treachery]] of the eyes, meaning the evil glance. See [[passage/quran-33-72|al-Ahzab, verse 72]], [[passage/quran-40-19|Ghafir, verse 19]] and [[passage/quran-11-111|Hud, verse 111]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:15:30}
- He relates by sense that among the gravest betrayals before God is for a man to disclose his wife's secret to the neighbour women, or a woman her husband's, and mentions two women who were married to two righteous servants and betrayed them, saying God called it treachery. See [[passage/quran-66-10|al-Tahrim, verse 10]]. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:16:30}
- He says one who does not use his reason falls into these five traits, and that the three roots of evil have branches: their holder lengthens sleep, eating and rest and avoids effort, becomes domineering even over a small post, and loves praise, boasting and being magnified by people. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:17:30}
- He relates by sense a report about a man of the fire seated with men standing before him, says the Companions loved to stand for the Prophet and he forbade them, and mentions that the Prophet forbade being extolled as the Christians extolled the son of Mary, or magnified as the non-Arabs magnified their kings, and told them to say the servant of God and His messenger. See [[passage/quran-83-6|al-Mutaffifin, verse 6]]. (needs_source: the reports are by sense and without a chain, and the first is garbled in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:18:30}
- He says God chained the devils in [[concept/ramadan-month|Ramadan]] so that the one who does good may advance and the one who does evil may stop, and that a person cannot see [[person/iblis|Satan]] and so turns to the One who sees Satan and is not seen by him, asking refuge from the whisperings of the devils and from their presence. He explains that the formula of [[concept/taawwudh|seeking refuge]] begins with the All-Hearing, All-Knowing because He hears the whispers and knows how grave a suggestion is. See [[passage/quran-23-97|al-Mu'minun, verse 97]] and [[passage/quran-23-98|al-Mu'minun, verse 98]]. (needs_source: the chaining of the devils is given by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:19:30}
- He mentions [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] in the cave saying that if one of them looked under his feet he would see them, and the Prophet's answer that God was with them. He says it was the company of the One who is not seen, so no one saw them, that [[person/abu-jahl|Abu Jahl]] and those at the cave's mouth would have taken them had they seen them, and holds that the One who gave sight took it away at that moment. See [[passage/quran-9-40|al-Tawba, verse 40]], [[passage/quran-6-103|al-An'am, verse 103]] and [[passage/quran-7-198|al-A'raf, verse 198]]. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain, and Abu Jahl at the cave mouth is his own addition) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:21:00}
- He says God gave fire its power to burn and withheld it from [[person/ibrahim|Ibrahim]], and that the verse joined cold with safety because cold alone can kill. He mentions bitter cold in northern Scandinavia, Canada and Siberia, says the zamharir is a torment in the fire that makes flesh fall from the bone and that the jinn are tormented by it because they are made of fire, notes that the people of Paradise see neither sun nor zamharir there, and says that after some days they found only the bonds had burned. See [[passage/quran-21-69|al-Anbiya, verse 69]] and [[passage/quran-76-13|al-Insan, verse 13]]. (needs_source: the temperatures are given with no source, the torment of the jinn by zamharir is his own view, and the burning of the bonds alone has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:23:30}
- He tells that [[person/ahmad-ibn-tulun|Ibn Tulun]] condemned Abu al-Hasan the ascetic to be eaten by a starved lion, that the lion came and sniffed at his garment, and that when Ibn Tulun asked what he had been thinking he said he was wondering whether the lion's leavings were pure or impure, adding that hearts are with God. (needs_source: the story is without a chain and the name stands as in the machine transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:24:30}
- He says God removed the knife's power to cut when Ibrahim set out to slaughter his son and ransomed him with a great sacrifice, removed the sea's power to drown in the case of the infant his mother cast into it, and removed the whale's power to digest in the case of [[person/yunus|Yunus]]. He concludes that a person should place his certainty in God and intervene in events only by doing the causes of good, for one who sows evil does not reap good. This is his conclusion. See [[passage/quran-37-107|al-Saffat, verse 107]], [[passage/quran-28-7|al-Qasas, verse 7]], [[passage/quran-37-143|al-Saffat, verse 143]] and [[passage/quran-37-144|al-Saffat, verse 144]]. (needs_source: the reading of the sea and the infant's mother comes from a garbled transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:25:30}
- He urges a person to be [[person/habil|Habil]] and not [[person/qabil|Qabil]], to be killed and not a killer, wronged and not a wrongdoer, and tells that one of Adam's two sons offered the worst of his plants and fruits and the other the finest ram of the flock, that the offering of one was accepted and the other's was not, and that envy then set to work. See [[passage/quran-5-27|al-Ma'ida, verse 27]]. (needs_source: the details of the offering are not in the verse and have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:27:00}
- He says the courts are full of the spiteful and the envious and asks what they gain, calls for using reason to bring people happiness and for the good word to be a charity, and advises a husband not to threaten his wife each day that he will marry again, and to sit half an hour with his children instead of at the cafe. This is his opinion. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:28:00}
- He relates that [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] said that if a mule stumbled on the bank of the Euphrates he would fear God would hold him to account for it, and that [[person/umar-ibn-abd-al-aziz|Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz]] instructed his governor in Egypt on the weight of the bit in the mouths of animals. He says that in Egypt in the fourth century of the Hijra there was a [[concept/waqf|waqf]] for stray dogs, cats and aged animals, and a waqf of yoghurt bowls under which a servant who broke a bowl was not harmed. (needs_source: reports without a chain, and the endowments without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:29:00}
- He says people used to show mercy to one another and mercy came down from the sky, notes that God has prescribed [[concept/rahma|mercy]] upon Himself, relates by sense a divine saying in which God forbids wrongdoing to Himself and makes it forbidden among people, and advises walking in the space of pardon, giving the example of a man who spends ten years in court over five metres of land while the lawyers' fees exceed its price. See [[passage/quran-6-54|al-An'am, verse 54]]. (needs_source: the divine saying is by sense and without a chain, and the court example is his own) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:30:00}
- He relates that whoever leaves something for God, God compensates him with better, and criticises mutual envy and hatred, the bribe-taker, the one who will not work and the one who sleeps in Ramadan and then wants mercy to come down. He says one given wealth should spend from it, one given knowledge should counsel, and one given standing should use it to open people's homes. This is his opinion. (needs_source: the saying on leaving something for God is given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:30:30}
- He says Satan is an absent enemy while the Muslim has a present beloved in God, and closes by saying that those who did not use their reason or listen with the ears of the heart to the preachers and the words of the prophets are the people of the blaze whose admission the verses record. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep15 00:31:00}

## Episode 16: justice, excellence, giving to kin, and the prohibition of indecency, wrongdoing and transgression
- He pauses at the verse of Surat al-Nahl that many preachers use to close the Friday sermon, saying it is so comprehensive in its commands and prohibitions that it would suffice anyone who took it as the programme of his life, and that the human being came down to earth to build it up through piety with God's way in hand. See [[passage/quran-16-90|al-Nahl, verse 90]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:02:00}
- He says Islam came when the Arab environment despised women, some tribes buried girls alive and most gave women no inheritance, and that the Qur'an startled them by treating righteous work of male or female alike and giving each a share of what they earned. He likens male and female to the two wings of a bird, and says they are equal before God though different in tasks because of their nature. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-16-97|al-Nahl, verse 97]], [[passage/quran-3-195|Al Imran, verse 195]] and [[passage/quran-4-32|al-Nisa, verse 32]]. (needs_source: his generalisation about Arab society before Islam has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:04:00}
- He says Islam came when the Arabs looked down on work and called it a degrading trade, and that the Qur'an drew them to the value of [[concept/amal|work]]. He relates by sense that God loves it when one of you does a job to do it well, that the Prophet saw a man digging a grave and said make it good, and that he told a Companion with rough hands that it was a hand God and His Messenger love. See [[passage/quran-9-105|al-Tawba, verse 105]] and [[passage/quran-34-13|Saba, verse 13]]. The subject is connected to [[concept/itqan-fil-amal|excellence in work]]. (needs_source: the sayings and reports are by sense and without a chain, and his generalisation about the Arabs has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:05:30}
- He tells from memory the [[event/harb-al-basus|War of al-Basus]], saying al-Basus's she-camel entered Kulayb's protected pasture and Jassas stabbed his sister's husband Kulayb, so the war between the two tribes lasted forty years. He also mentions the War of Dahis and al-Ghabra that began with a horse race rigged by a trick, says their poet declared they would fight their brother Bakr if they found no one else, and that they said they would begin as wrongdoers, and he faults this logic. See [[person/kulayb-ibn-rabia|Kulayb ibn Rabia]]. (needs_source: the details of both tales are from memory without attribution, and the verses are unattributed) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:09:30}
- He says the traits of the believers are that they are humble toward believers and firm against unbelievers, and that they are brothers, and relates by sense that the Prophet said that had he taken a human being as an intimate friend he would have taken Abu Bakr, but your companion is the intimate friend of the Merciful. See [[passage/quran-5-54|al-Ma'ida, verse 54]] and [[passage/quran-49-10|al-Hujurat, verse 10]]. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:10:00}
- He says the Roman, Greek, Indian and Persian civilisations began to fall when their moral side collapsed, and that Islam revived hearts with values. He notes that the verse joins three commands and three prohibitions, that [[concept/adl|justice]] is the first thing it commands, and that it is the foundation of rule. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-16-90|al-Nahl, verse 90]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:11:00}
- He relates by sense that judges are of three kinds and two of them are in the fire, and mentions that Islam barred testimony of a descendant for an ascendant or the reverse, of a husband for his wife and of a grandfather for his grandson because of the inclination of the heart, saying a person cannot control the heart's inclination but is bound to treat people equally in what he gives. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain, and the ruling on testimony is given without naming a school) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:12:00}
- He relates by sense that the Prophet divided his time among his wives and would say this is my division in what I own, so do not blame me in what You own and I do not, and says the heart cannot be owned, so a father may incline to one child yet must be equal in what he gives. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:13:00}
- He tells of a Companion, named Bashir in the transcript, who came to the Prophet to have him witness a garden he had given to his son, and the Prophet asked whether he had given the same to all his sons, he said no, and the Prophet said would it please him to have them equally dutiful, and that he would not bear witness to injustice. He relates by sense that one who is unfair among his children will come on the Day of Resurrection with one side tilted. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain, and the name stands as in the machine transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:15:30}
- He speaks of bank loans, saying a man who borrows a hundred thousand for a car repays far more, that this is destruction and war from God and His Messenger, and that the war shows in diseases, high prices and injustice. This is his opinion. He draws attention to [[concept/riba|usury]]. See [[passage/quran-2-276|al-Baqara, verse 276]] and [[passage/quran-2-279|al-Baqara, verse 279]]. (needs_source: the figures have no source and part of the text is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:14:30}
- He says the accused is innocent until clear evidence appears as plain as the sun, that erring in pardon is better than erring in punishment, and that jailing someone unjustly even for a day is wrong. He relates by sense that a woman entered the fire over a cat, mentions that God honoured the children of Adam and that the Prophet forbade striking the face, and says slapping a child's face kills four hundred cells of the brain while stroking an orphan's head revives four hundred, with a good deed for every hair. See [[passage/quran-17-70|al-Isra, verse 70]]. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain, the four hundred cells is a figure with no source, and the saying on pardon is unattributed) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:17:30}
- He tells that al-Shafi'i used to ask two litigants whether he should judge between them by justice or by grace, saying justice is that each take his right and grace is that each forgives the other, then tells of two men of the Children of Israel, one of whom bought a house and found a jar of gold in its ground; neither buyer nor seller would take it, so the judge married the son of one to the daughter of the other and made it the dower, and their descendants were righteous. He takes it as a sign that when righteousness spreads greed lessens. (needs_source: both reports are without a chain, and the first is credited to al-Shafi'i with no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:20:00}
- He says [[concept/ihsan|excellence]] covers everything, even slaughter and killing, and relates by sense that the Prophet commanded doing slaughter well, sharpening the blade and not slaughtering before another animal, says killing a rabid dog is mercy, and relates by sense that whoever kills a lizard with the first blow has three hundred good deeds, fewer with the second and third. (needs_source: the reports are by sense and without a chain, and the numbers of good deeds are as he gave them) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:21:30}
- He relates by sense the report of the man who watered a thirsty dog from a well with his shoe and was forgiven, and of the man who cut a thorny branch from the road and was admitted to Paradise, then asks about those who cut fathers, mothers and sons from their families and throw them into prisons, and calls for reviewing accounts. This is his opinion. (needs_source: the reports are by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:23:00}
- He says excellence goes beyond justice, mentions the words of the people of [[person/qarun|Qarun]] that he should do good as God did good to him and not seek corruption, and says the corrupter is one who abolished justice and excellence, citing the Qur'an's description of Pharaoh as one of the corrupters. See [[passage/quran-28-77|al-Qasas, verse 77]], [[passage/quran-7-56|al-A'raf, verse 56]] and [[passage/quran-28-4|al-Qasas, verse 4]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:24:30}
- He explains giving to kin as the good word and keeping family ties, says a paternal uncle and aunt come from the father's mercy and a maternal uncle and aunt from the mother's, and relates by sense that a man of many sins asked the Prophet about repentance and was asked about his mother and then his aunt and told to be good to her. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:25:30}
- He says the world wants to bring us back to the law of the jungle and to pre-Islamic ignorance, and that when a believer dies the place of his prostration on earth and the place where his deeds ascend in heaven weep for him, unlike others of whom it is said they are at rest and others are at rest from them, relating this report by sense. This is his opinion. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:26:30}
- He says he loves the Companions though he never saw them and does not know their colour or height, and contrasts those called former president, Roosevelt and Churchill with Abu Hanifa, of whom one never says former, saying people are dead and the people of knowledge alive. This is his opinion. (needs_source: the saying that people are dead and scholars alive is unattributed) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:28:00}
- He explains that indecency is whatever is foul in word or deed, relates by sense that the believer is neither foul nor vulgar, and that the abomination is whatever the law, sound nature, minds and clean hearts reject, and says the good word is charity and a gentle tongue prevails. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:29:30}
- He applies the rule of the [[concept/munkar|abomination]] to weddings, mentioning dancing and the mixing of men and women, and relates by sense that whoever pleases God at the cost of people's displeasure, God is pleased with him and makes people pleased with him. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain, and his position on wedding customs is garbled in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:30:30}
- He says transgression, wrongdoing and disobedience to parents have their penalty hastened in this world, calls the verse an admonition for those of sound mind, and suggests a believer take it as a programme of action: command justice and be just, command excellence and be excellent, keep ties with his kin and refrain from indecency and the abomination. This is his opinion. (needs_source: the wording on hastened punishment is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:32:30}
- He says one who is present, under necessity, where there is an abomination and rejects it in his heart is as one who was not there, while one who is absent and approves is as one who was present. He relates by sense that God told Gabriel to overturn a town in which there was a pious servant and to begin with him because his face never frowned, cites the cursing of the Children of Israel through David and Jesus for not forbidding a wrong they did, and relates by sense that a community that does not say to the wrongdoer you are a wrongdoer is as good as lost. See [[passage/quran-5-78|al-Ma'ida, verse 78]] and [[passage/quran-5-79|al-Ma'ida, verse 79]]. (needs_source: the reports are by sense and without a chain, and the story of Gabriel has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:34:30}
- He says the Muslim's measure is the Book and the Sunna and that one lives once, so passivity is not allowed. He gives the example of one who enters a land where people worship the calf and does not feed it with them, and answers the saying please them while you are on their land and humour them while you are in their house by saying it does not mean drinking or dancing with them, and that the truth is spoken whole. This is his opinion. (needs_source: the quoted saying is given without attribution) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:36:00}
- He tells that [[person/abu-talib|Abu Talib]] said to the Prophet that his call had divided father from son and husband from wife, offering him wealth, kingship and a physician, and that the Prophet replied that if they put the sun in his right hand and the moon in his left to make him abandon this matter he would not abandon it until God made it prevail or he perished in it, and the lecturer adds that the Prophet then had only [[person/khadija|Khadija]], the young Ali and Abu Bakr with him. (needs_source: the report is without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:37:00}
- He says one who is in God's company need not fear, and relates by sense a counsel that what befell you could not have missed you, and that victory comes with patience and relief with hardship. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:37:30}
- He closes with prayers and hopes for his listeners in Syria, Iraq, Libya, Yemen, Egypt and Palestine for security and for the return of the displaced and refugees to their homes. He says that when Egypt is strong Islam is strong, and that Umar restored [[place/jerusalem|Jerusalem]] and then [[person/salah-al-din-yusuf-ibn-ayyub|Salah al-Din]] after an occupation of about ninety-two years. This is his opinion and hope. (needs_source: the length of the occupation and the claim about Egypt are his own) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep16 00:40:30}

## Episode 17: God's promise to the oppressed and the leadership of the subdued
- He pauses at the verse of Surat al-Qasas on God's wish to favour those who were made weak in the land, make them leaders and make them the inheritors. He says that when oppression multiplies in a world, mercy is withdrawn from it, and relates by sense that whoever shows mercy to those on earth is shown mercy by Him who is in heaven. See [[passage/quran-28-5|al-Qasas, verse 5]]. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:01:30}
- He says Islam took care of the weak whoever they are: the elderly are put first and the young have their rights, and he relates by sense that he is not of us who does not honour our elder and show mercy to our young, and that the Prophet said fear God regarding the two weak ones, meaning the orphan and the woman. (needs_source: the reports are by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:02:30}
- He says that however formidable a woman may be she has a simple key, and quotes the sages as saying that if she puts you in her heart she will raise you to the heights but if she puts you in her knot no one can help you. (needs_source: the saying is credited to unnamed sages) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:02:30}
- He advises wives on dealing with a husband's mother, saying the mother raised her son and when she visits and sits in the guest room like a stranger she feels weak and sad, and that a wise wife brings her into the family sitting room and tells her this is your house and we are your guests, citing a line of verse on Arab hospitality to the guest. This is his opinion and advice. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:07:00}
- He says a woman protected by her husband is strong, but when widowed she becomes weak and people keep away, so society should look after the widow and her orphans, and a divorced woman should not become prey for anyone but be treated as a daughter, sister or cousin. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:08:30}
- He relates by sense that those nearest the Prophet in seat on the Day of Resurrection are the kindest to their wives, that the best house before God is one in which an orphan is honoured, that when an orphan cries God asks the angels who made him cry and that whoever quiets him earns His favour and enters Paradise, and that the guardian of an orphan and the Prophet are in Paradise like these two. He advises inviting an orphan to break the fast in Ramadan, giving new clothes at the feast and not adding the misfortune of poverty to that of loss. (needs_source: the reports are by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:11:00}
- He says the community was described through its messenger, whom God sent as a mercy to the worlds, so it too should be a mercy to trees and water. He mentions that Islam forbade cutting trees and relieving oneself in their shade or in standing water, and relates that Abu Bakr instructed the armies not to cut a tree nor to kill an old man, a woman or a child. See [[passage/quran-21-107|al-Anbiya, verse 107]]. (needs_source: the reports are by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:11:30}
- He says that when [[person/salah-al-din-yusuf-ibn-ayyub|Salah al-Din]] received Jerusalem after ninety-two years of Crusader occupation he spared the remaining Crusaders and gave them money to return home, whereas he says the Crusaders killed seventy thousand Muslims on the day they entered, and that the Copts of Egypt took refuge with Amr ibn al-As against their Roman rulers. This is his opinion. (needs_source: the figures, the length of the occupation and the claim about the Copts have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:12:30}
- He takes up the words of Shu'ayb's people that they would expel him and those with him or he must return to their creed, then Moses telling his people that the earth is God's and He gives it to whom He wishes and the outcome is for the pious. He says geographic borders are a human making, and that those made weak who did not establish their religion are asked whether God's earth was not wide enough to emigrate in. This is his explanation. See [[passage/quran-7-88|al-A'raf, verse 88]], [[passage/quran-7-128|al-A'raf, verse 128]] and [[passage/quran-4-97|al-Nisa, verse 97]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:14:30}
- He notes that one's homeland is dear and quotes a line saying his land, even when it wrongs him, is dear, then says that one who feels injustice and exile in his own land may travel, citing verses he credits to al-Shafi'i on travel. (needs_source: the verses are credited to al-Shafi'i with no source, and the first line is unattributed) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:15:30}
- He mentions [[person/sumayya-bint-khayyat|Sumayya]] and [[person/yasir-ibn-amir|Yasir]] and their killing by the persecutors, and that [[person/ammar-ibn-yasir|Ammar]] was young and saw his mother killed. He then says the Prophet entered at the conquest reciting the verse on truth arriving and falsehood vanishing, concludes that the oppressed will win one day and urges being wronged rather than wrongdoing, and relates by sense that the prayer of the wronged is not rejected even if he is an unbeliever. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-17-81|al-Isra, verse 81]]. (needs_source: the killer's name is garbled in the transcript, and the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:16:30}
- He says the weak whom God promised leadership are the inheritors who inherit Paradise, and that the inheritance of Paradise has no dispute, no salty land and no building that falls. This is his explanation. See [[passage/quran-23-10|al-Mu'minun, verse 10]] and [[passage/quran-23-11|al-Mu'minun, verse 11]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:18:00}
- He tells that a king built a palace and asked about its flaw, and an old man told him it had two: its ruin, however late, and the death of its owner, however late. He says that when the mummy of Ramesses II was taken to France archaeologists established that he died by drowning, which fits what the Qur'an says of Pharaoh and his troops drowning. See [[passage/quran-28-40|al-Qasas, verse 40]]. (needs_source: the story has no chain, and the statement about archaeologists has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:19:30}
- He says a human being owns neither a thread, a speck nor a husk, nor death, life or resurrection, and explains the three words as the thread in the groove of the date stone, the dot on its back and the thin skin around it. See [[passage/quran-25-3|al-Furqan, verse 3]], [[passage/quran-4-49|al-Nisa, verse 49]], [[passage/quran-4-53|al-Nisa, verse 53]] and [[passage/quran-35-13|Fatir, verse 13]]. (needs_source: the sense given to the three words has no attribution) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:21:30}
