- He tells that [[person/kafur-al-ikhshidi|Kafur]] was a slave and prisoner taken in chains to Egypt, who passed a kebab seller near the mosque of Amr ibn al-As and wished to work for him and eat meat free, then said his wish was to rule Egypt, and later ruled it and founded the Ikhshidid state, and that [[person/al-mutanabbi|al-Mutanabbi]] satirised him when he did not honour him, on the road to Syria. He takes the slave's ambition as an illustration that whoever wants this world is given of it and whoever wants the hereafter and strives for it has his striving thanked. See [[passage/quran-17-18|al-Isra, verse 18]], [[passage/quran-17-19|al-Isra, verse 19]] and [[passage/quran-17-20|al-Isra, verse 20]]. (needs_source: the story has no chain and its details are from memory) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:24:30}
- He advises a person not to occupy himself with envying others but to ask the Owner of the treasuries, notes that God invites a goodly loan and that people are the poor before Him, and says one with a disobedient son or a rebellious daughter should pray to God and make sure what they are fed and clothed with is lawful. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-2-245|al-Baqara, verse 245]] and [[passage/quran-35-15|Fatir, verse 15]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:26:30}
- He says the first thing a servant is asked about on the Day of Resurrection is the bounty, that God's blessings cannot be counted, that a person forgets thirty years of health at the first illness, that a trial may rouse him to give thanks, and that among the blessings are Islam, a righteous child, a patient wife, the movement of the body and God's covering of faults. He relates that an imam asked about the bounty said cold water in summer, and relates by sense that if the son of Adam had two valleys of gold he would seek a third and only dust fills his belly. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-102-8|al-Takathur, verse 8]] and [[passage/quran-14-34|Ibrahim, verse 34]]. (needs_source: the imam is not named, and the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:29:00}
- He says empowerment on earth is in God's hand, mentions Pharaoh killing the males of the Children of Israel and drowning with his troops, and mentions [[person/qarun|Qarun]], saying he was of Moses's people and wronged them, withheld zakat and turned base metals into precious ones. See [[passage/quran-28-76|al-Qasas, verse 76]]. (needs_source: the turning of metals and the withholding of zakat have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:30:30}
- He explains the words of Qarun's people that he should not exult, distinguishing a blameworthy joy in the gift without the Giver from a praiseworthy joy in God's bounty and mercy, in obedience, and in a Muslim's marriage and provision. He then recounts Qarun coming out in his finery, those who wanted this life wishing for the like, those with knowledge saying God's reward is better, the earth swallowing him and his house, and those who had wished for his place saying so the day before. See [[passage/quran-28-79|al-Qasas, verse 79]], [[passage/quran-28-80|al-Qasas, verse 80]], [[passage/quran-28-81|al-Qasas, verse 81]], [[passage/quran-28-82|al-Qasas, verse 82]] and [[passage/quran-28-83|al-Qasas, verse 83]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:35:00}
- He says Muslims and Arabs put barriers in front of the successful among them until they fail, and that this is not a trait of believers. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:34:00}
- He relates by sense that three follow the dead and two return while one remains: his wealth and family return and his deed remains, that a righteous deed meets its owner in the grave in a fair form and an evil deed in a gloomy one, and he seeks refuge from the gloom of the sight. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:37:30}
- He says empowerment comes through obedience and gives a saying he credits to [[person/ibn-taymiyya|Ibn Taymiyya]] that God supports a just state even if unbelieving and defeats an unjust state even if Muslim, relating by sense that wrongdoing is darkness on the Day of Resurrection, and says justice is the foundation of rule. (needs_source: the saying is credited to Ibn Taymiyya with no source, and the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:38:30}
- He mentions Pharaoh, Haman and their troops, answers one who says he is a servant under orders that he must refuse, and says that whoever sides with a wrongdoer knowing him to be one has left the religion and that this is the ruling of the law. This is his statement and ruling. See [[passage/quran-28-6|al-Qasas, verse 6]]. (needs_source: the ruling is given with no source and no school named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:39:00}
- He closes saying the earth is God's and He gives it to whom He wishes, and that the oppressed have a strong Lord who will support them at the time He wills and not the time they will. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep17 00:39:30}

## Episode 18: the day the wrongdoer bites his hands and the choice of a close friend
- He pauses at the verses of Surat al-Furqan on the day the sky splits with clouds and the angels come down, when dominion that day is truly the Merciful's and it is a hard day for the unbelievers, and the wrongdoer bites his hands wishing he had taken a path with the Messenger and not taken a certain man as a close friend. See [[passage/quran-25-25|al-Furqan, verse 25]], [[passage/quran-25-26|al-Furqan, verse 26]], [[passage/quran-25-27|al-Furqan, verse 27]], [[passage/quran-25-28|al-Furqan, verse 28]] and [[passage/quran-25-29|al-Furqan, verse 29]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:02:00}
- He quotes a received saying that this world is an hour so make it obedience, and says four matters enter a person's creed: no rest in this world, no trick in sustenance, no intercession in death and no turning back God's decree. (needs_source: the saying is unattributed, and the four matters are his own) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:03:00}
- He says one who is not at peace with himself gives no peace to others, that peace in the world cannot be unless a person is at peace with himself and his society, relates by sense that the Prophet said the best of you is the best to his family and I am the best to my family, and likens one who trusts the world to a man gripping water that escapes between his fingers. This is his opinion. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:04:30}
- He describes the Day of Resurrection as the Qur'an presents it: the sky splits with clouds and the angels come down and encircle the people of the gathering so no one escapes or finds refuge, then mentions the angel of death, the barzakh, and the two blasts of the trumpet and people standing and looking on. The subject is connected to [[concept/yawm-al-qiyama|the Day of Resurrection]]. See [[passage/quran-39-68|al-Zumar, verse 68]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:07:00}
- He explains the choice of the name the Merciful in the verse on dominion that day as a name of mercy and not of force, relates by sense that God made mercy a hundred parts, sent one down to earth by which creatures show mercy so that even a beast lifts its hoof from its young, and kept ninety-nine for the Day of Resurrection. He describes a giraffe giving birth standing and the newborn rising within minutes, says the human newborn is the weakest, and holds that a person should thank God for His blessings and for creating him weak. See [[passage/quran-4-28|al-Nisa, verse 28]]. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain, and the giraffe description is from his own viewing) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:10:00}
- He says humanity has divided into a first and a last world, or north and south, that the West killed life and we killed creativity, and that civilisation lives only by creativity and life. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:11:00}
- He tells that the Commander of the Faithful heard thunder and was afraid, and one of the righteous told him this is the sound of His mercy, so what of the sound of His punishment. (needs_source: the story has no chain and names no one) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:11:30}
- He says God puts some people in charge of the necks of others who then imprison, torture and kill without right, explains unbelief as covering the natural disposition on which God created people, and says the day is hard on the unbeliever because there is no intercession, no return and no second court, for he wrote his own hereafter with his hand. He mentions that no soul bears another's burden and that there is no punishment before messengers are sent. See [[passage/quran-17-14|al-Isra, verse 14]] and [[passage/quran-17-15|al-Isra, verse 15]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:13:30}
- He cites the verse on God's wish to destroy a town and His command to its affluent ones who then act wickedly, and credits [[person/abdullah-ibn-abbas|Ibn Abbas]] with taking the affluent to be those given power who did not fear God. See [[passage/quran-17-16|al-Isra, verse 16]]. (needs_source: the explanation is credited to Ibn Abbas with no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:14:00}
- He tells of a group in the 1960s who turned their occupied country's cause from an Islamic one into an Arab nationalist one, and who went in 1969 to meet Mao Zedong, a communist and atheist. He says Mao told those hosting them that in Ramadan no food should be served by day and the alcohol removed from hotel fridges, and when he saw them asking for food, drink and alcohol in Ramadan he told them that a cause whose bearer contradicts in his conduct the customs and beliefs of his people will not triumph. (needs_source: the whole story has no chain and the group is not named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:16:00}
- He cites the words of [[person/bilqis|Bilqis]] that when kings enter a town they ruin it and make its mighty ones low, and that the Qur'an confirmed her with the words and so they do. See [[passage/quran-27-34|al-Naml, verse 34]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:16:30}
- He says the Arabs say nothing bites harder than the bite of regret, cites a poet's line on regret, and links it to the verse of the wrongdoer biting his hands, mentioning God's command to follow His straight path and not the other paths and that the sincere Muslim walks the white road. See [[passage/quran-6-153|al-An'am, verse 153]] and [[passage/quran-12-108|Yusuf, verse 108]]. (needs_source: the line of verse is unattributed) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:18:00}
- He says on the Day of Resurrection a person flees from his mother, father, brother, wife and sons, every soul saying myself, myself, and relates by sense that the Prophet alone says my community, my Lord. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-80-34|Abasa, verse 34]], [[passage/quran-80-35|Abasa, verse 35]], [[passage/quran-80-36|Abasa, verse 36]] and [[passage/quran-80-37|Abasa, verse 37]]. The subject is connected to [[concept/shafaa|intercession]]. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:18:30}
- He explains the close friend as one who permeates a person's life, relates by sense that the Prophet said had he taken a close friend he would have taken Abu Bakr but your companion is the Friend of the Merciful, and lists five traits of such a friend: his sight reminds you of God, his state points you to God, his speech adds to your knowledge, he reminds you if he sees you heedless and helps you if he sees you remembering. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain, and the five traits are given with no attribution) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:20:00}
- He says those who were followed disown their followers on the Day of Resurrection and the followers wish for a return to disown them in turn, that a person carries his own burdens and those of the people he led astray, and relates by sense the report on one who starts an evil practice. See [[passage/quran-2-166|al-Baqara, verse 166]], [[passage/quran-2-167|al-Baqara, verse 167]] and [[passage/quran-16-25|al-Nahl, verse 25]]. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:22:00}
- He says a person's real age is by the scale of deeds and not the count of days, and likens the heart to grammatical states: it is raised by remembrance, opened by contentment, broken, and then still, stopping in heedlessness. He links the raising to God's command to remember Him often and to His blessing the believers, with His angels, to bring them from darkness to light, and says hard hearts earn woe. This is his comparison. See [[passage/quran-33-41|al-Ahzab, verse 41]], [[passage/quran-33-42|al-Ahzab, verse 42]], [[passage/quran-33-43|al-Ahzab, verse 43]] and [[passage/quran-39-22|al-Zumar, verse 22]]. The subject is connected to [[concept/dhikr|remembrance]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:33:30}
- He lists the signs of a heart raised by remembrance: loving God's words, loving to sit with those who speak of them and with those who put them into practice, and not mocking them. He cites the verse in Surat al-Mutaffifin that the criminals used to laugh at the believers, and says that one who does not keep company with the good should at least not harm them. See [[passage/quran-83-29|al-Mutaffifin, verse 29]], [[passage/quran-83-30|al-Mutaffifin, verse 30]] and [[passage/quran-83-31|al-Mutaffifin, verse 31]]. (needs_source: the last saying is unattributed) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:25:30}
- He tells that [[person/ayyub|Ayyub]] said to Iblis that if he knew the blessing he was in he would envy him, meaning the blessing of [[concept/rida-bil-qada|contentment]] with God. He says the highest degree of contentment is for blessing and affliction to be equal to the servant because both are from God, likening it to a child pained by a doctor's injection while his father sees it as a blessing. See [[passage/quran-9-51|al-Tawba, verse 51]]. (needs_source: the exchange between Ayyub and Iblis has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:29:30}
- He recites lines saying destinies run on their reins and God changes state to state, and a saying that a person should not journey from creation to creation and be like a mill donkey but should journey from creations to the Creator, and likens being with God to walking with a head of state so that VIP lounges open to you, mentioning the Prophet's words to his companion in the cave that God is with us. See [[passage/quran-53-42|al-Najm, verse 42]] and [[passage/quran-9-40|al-Tawba, verse 40]]. (needs_source: the lines and the saying are unattributed) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:28:00}
- He says childlessness may be a gift from God because a child may be the reason his parent enters the fire, as Noah's son did, and that hearts are opened by contentment. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-42-50|al-Shura, verse 50]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:30:00}
- He says this world does not last for anyone, mentions the Romans, Persian emperors, Caesars, Pharaoh, Qarun and Haman, and mentions [[person/abu-jahl|Abu Jahl]] and the party he planned before Badr, then his killing and being thrown into the [[event/badr-qulayb|well]]. He relates by sense that the Prophet asked the people of the well whether they had found what their Lord promised true and that they hear his words but do not reply. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain, and the party is from memory) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:31:30}
- He says there is no law linking death with illness or age, for the sick and the healthy, old and young alike die. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:32:30}
- He says one absorbed in worldly affairs, the stock market, shares and property has a heedless heart that stops, that the owner of a stopped heart cannot even speak a kind word, and quotes scholars as saying soft speech overcomes clear truth. (needs_source: the saying is credited to unnamed scholars) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:34:00}
- He criticises Ramadan television serials, saying Arab satellite channels produced sixty in a year and that one who sat for thirteen months would not finish them, that God chains the devils of the jinn in Ramadan but the devils of mankind take their place, and relating by sense that in Ramadan the gates of Paradise open, the gates of the fire close, the devils are chained and a caller says seeker of good come forward and seeker of evil stop. He says blessing was lifted from the dinner table as rooftop dishes multiplied, and that meat, vegetables and chicken now carry pesticides and hormones. This is his opinion. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain, and the claims on pesticides, hormones and the number of serials have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:37:00}
- He says that in the 1940s and 1950s the community spent Ramadan without puzzles or serials, that each person should begin with himself, and that the true friend is one who tells you the truth and not one who tells you you are right. This is his opinion. (needs_source: his saying on the friend is unattributed) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:39:00}
- He mentions the expelled and refugees whom the hand of oppression drove from their lands, not for any wrong of theirs but because of the existence of injustice and the wrongdoer, then returns to the verse of the wrongdoer wishing he had not taken a certain man as a close friend and says the devil of mankind comes before the devil of the jinn. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:40:00}
- He says some people choose from religion what pleases them, loving fasting but not prayer or loving prayer and fasting but not the headscarf, likens it to those who believe in part of the Book and reject part, calls it an individual religion, and concludes that a believer should take from the episode to choose a friend who reminds him if he sees him heedless and helps him if he sees him remembering. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-2-85|al-Baqara, verse 85]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep18 00:41:30}

## Episode 19: prayer upon the Prophet as a prayer of following and not of the tongue
- He pauses at the verse of Surat al-Ahzab that God and His angels send blessings on the Prophet and bids the believers do so and greet him, links it to the verse of love that came down when people claimed to love God, and says a statement in the Qur'an carries a command within it and that the call is addressed to the believers in particular. See [[passage/quran-33-56|al-Ahzab, verse 56]] and [[passage/quran-3-31|Al Imran, verse 31]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:03:00}
- He says the believers thereby resemble the angels, whose tasks include glorifying God and praying upon the Prophet, mentions that some orientalists say God had the angels prostrate to Adam while they extol the Prophet, and answers that the angels' prostration to Adam was a moment in time whereas their prayer on the Prophet lasts through all time. See [[passage/quran-2-34|al-Baqara, verse 34]]. (needs_source: the orientalists' claim is unnamed and the text is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:05:00}
- He says God honoured His Prophet with many verses, among them the Qur'an coming down on him though it would have split a mountain, his being called a mercy to the worlds and a person of great character. In his view the highest is the verse telling him to say that if the Merciful had a son he would be the first to worship. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-59-21|al-Hashr, verse 21]], [[passage/quran-21-107|al-Anbiya, verse 107]], [[passage/quran-68-4|al-Qalam, verse 4]] and [[passage/quran-43-81|al-Zukhruf, verse 81]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:06:30}
- He says prayer in the language means doing good, that the Arabs used the word for the nerves and vessels around the spine so that it is like the support on which a person moves, and cites half a line of verse in which the leader among horses is followed by the one called al-musalli, the follower, from which he takes it that praying upon the Prophet means following him. The subject is connected to [[concept/ittiba|following]]. (needs_source: the linguistic derivations have no attribution and the line of verse is unattributed) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:15:00}
- He relates by sense that whoever prays upon the Prophet once, God rewards him for it ten times, and that God returns the Prophet's spirit so that he answers the one who greets him. He says the word is shared but its meaning differs, so God's prayer on His Prophet, the angels' prayer and ours are different in sense and only God knows them. (needs_source: the reports are by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:08:00}
- He says God and His angels also bless the believers to bring them from darkness to light, lists the fruits of God's prayer upon us as mercy, forgiveness, guidance, more certainty, good character and benefit to others, says the bearers of the throne ask forgiveness for the believers, and that the angels say amen to our supplication so that when a servant's amen matches theirs God answers. See [[passage/quran-33-43|al-Ahzab, verse 43]] and [[passage/quran-40-7|Ghafir, verse 7]]. (needs_source: the fruits he lists are his own, and the report on the angels' amen is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:10:30}
- He says the verse holds several matters: raising the believers to the rank of the angels who pray, that the sinless angels busy with glorification also pray upon this human of Adam's line about whose kind they once asked whether they would spread corruption, and that all people, whatever their state, hasten to pray on him when he is mentioned. See [[passage/quran-2-30|al-Baqara, verse 30]]. This is his explanation. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:12:30}
- He advises one who supplicates to begin by praising God, then pray on the Prophet, then ask, and close with a prayer on him, saying God accepts the first and the last and is too generous to reject what lies between. (needs_source: the order is given with no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:13:30}
- He says we love God for His gifts, bounty and kindness, and that were destiny to run as people wish their affairs would be ruined. He relates by sense that if people relied on God as they should He would provide for them as He provides for birds that go out hungry and return full, and says the birds work the means by setting out early and striving. The subject is connected to [[concept/tawakkul|reliance on God]]. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:17:00}
- He quotes one of the righteous as saying my Lord told me be a servant, so I was a servant and found rest, takes it as a passport into the house of contentment, and links it to the angels telling the people of Paradise peace be upon you for your patience, saying they are patient under God's flowing decree. See [[passage/quran-13-24|al-Ra'd, verse 24]]. (needs_source: the saying is credited to an unnamed righteous man) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:18:30}
- He describes crossing the [[concept/sirat|Sirat]] over Hell as being like a sword's edge, says some scholars mention that Hell was kindled for a thousand years until red, a thousand until white and a thousand until black, and that people cross by the light of their deeds, one with light on his big toe, another like the moon, another like the sun, at speeds like lightning, a horse, a ram or a hen. He adds that the scratched are caught by hooks like thorns of the sa'dan plant, each sin having its hook, and the overturned is one whose evil outweighed his good. See [[passage/quran-21-104|al-Anbiya, verse 104]]. (needs_source: the reports are by sense and without a chain, the scholars are unnamed, and the speeds are his own) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:25:00}
- He says that one whose face is bright is one who prays often upon the Prophet, that some people mention him only on a single occasion, and he recommends the Ibrahimic formula as the best wording while holding any lawful formula sufficient. This is his opinion. (needs_source: his saying on the light of the face and his preference for the Ibrahimic formula have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:26:30}
- He relates from Aisha that a month or two would pass in the Prophet's house with no fire lit, their food being the two blacks, dates and water, then contrasts this with our abundance and says our children and wives do not give thanks because faith has weakened in hearts. This is his opinion. The subject is connected to [[person/aisha|Aisha]]. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:28:00}
- He says true love is practical, so a husband should follow the Prophet as husband, a teacher as teacher, a neighbour as neighbour and a father as father. He mentions that he fathered four daughters and sons who died in his lifetime except Fatima, who lived six months after him, that he rose for her when she came, that he prolonged his prostration while al-Hasan and al-Husayn climbed on his back, and says he took part in twenty-eight expeditions. The subject is connected to [[concept/mahabbat-al-nabi|love of the Prophet]]. (needs_source: the reports are by sense and without a chain, and the number of expeditions is as he gave it) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:30:00}
- He relates by sense that despite being protected from sin the Prophet sought forgiveness a hundred times a day, said when asked shall I not be a grateful servant, and said his heart was at times veiled, and he takes this seeking of forgiveness as a kind of thanks. The subject is connected to [[concept/istighfar|seeking forgiveness]]. (needs_source: the reports are by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:31:30}
- He says God commanded us a practical prayer, which is following, and asks whether the Prophet would waste his time on Ramadan serials and leave prayer, remembrance and the Qur'an, whether our fatherhood, marriage and neighbourliness match his, and whether the place of our prostration on earth and the place our deeds rise to in heaven will weep for us, adding that deeds end at death except three. This is his opinion. (needs_source: the saying and report are by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:33:30}
- He lists other worlds around us, mentioning the hoopoe calling to God, the ant that warned her kind of Solomon's army so that he smiled at her words, and the bee that eats only what is good and does not break the twig it lands on. See [[passage/quran-27-24|al-Naml, verse 24]], [[passage/quran-27-25|al-Naml, verse 25]], [[passage/quran-27-18|al-Naml, verse 18]] and [[passage/quran-27-19|al-Naml, verse 19]]. (needs_source: his description of the bee has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:35:00}
- He says one who knows everything but does not know his Lord has known nothing, that the deprived one is he who is denied the sweetness of knowing and loving God, and that one who prolongs prayers on the Prophet and neglects the obligatory prayers gains nothing, for real prayer upon him is the prayer of following and not of speech. This is his opinion. (needs_source: the first saying is unattributed) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep19 00:37:30}

## Episode 20: the one whose evil deed was made to look fair to him
- He pauses at the verse of Surat Fatir on one whose evil deed was made to look fair so that he sees it as good, that God leads astray whom He wills and guides whom He wills, and that one should not waste oneself in sorrow over them. He says the danger is that the scale of seeing is overturned, giving the example of a boy caught cheating in an exam whose father blames the invigilator, and says such a cheat may become a cheating doctor or engineer. See [[passage/quran-35-8|Fatir, verse 8]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:03:00}
- He relates by sense that one with an atom's weight of [[concept/kibr|arrogance]] in his heart will not enter Paradise, and defines arrogance as not accepting the truth and not obeying God by taking part of the Book and the Sunna and leaving part, so that one accepts prayer and fasting but rejects zakat or the headscarf. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:05:00}
- He likens one who takes part of the religion and rejects part to a parent who enrols his son in a school and tells its head he does not want languages and sciences for him, so the head refuses him, and says this is the state of those who believe in part of the Book and disbelieve in part. This is his comparison. See [[passage/quran-2-85|al-Baqara, verse 85]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:06:30}
- He says some women refuse the headscarf because they met harshness from a father, brother or husband, and answers that a woman's submission is to God and not to the husband, so one who submits to the Lord of the husband obeys the husband, citing that God burdens no soul beyond its capacity and that they do not believe until they make the Messenger their judge. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-2-286|al-Baqara, verse 286]] and [[passage/quran-4-65|al-Nisa, verse 65]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:09:00}
- He quotes one of the righteous as saying I have killed things and revived things, killing the self and reviving the heart, and says the self swells until its owner sees people as ants. He relates that [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] after the pilgrimage said he used to graze the camels of al-Khattab, who was harsh, and now he is Commander of the Faithful, and that his son told him his self wished to elevate itself so he wished to put it down. (needs_source: the saying is credited to an unnamed righteous man, and the report on Umar has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:10:30}
- He says [[person/khalid-ibn-al-walid|Khalid ibn al-Walid]] fought about a hundred battles and was never defeated, relates that at his death in Homs he said there was no spot on his body without a stab or blow and here he was dying in bed so may the eyes of cowards not sleep, says the bravest commanders of the Prophet's time and after were not granted martyrdom, and that the first expedition after the Hijra was led by Abu Bakr. (needs_source: the figures, the words at death and Abu Bakr as leader of the first expedition have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:12:00}
- He relates by sense that the Prophet found Abu Bakr praying in a low voice and Umar praying aloud, asked Abu Bakr and was told he was making his petition heard by the One he addressed, asked Umar and was told he was waking the drowsy and driving off Satan, and told Abu Bakr to raise his voice a little and Umar to lower his, and credits [[person/ibn-qayyim-al-jawziyya|Ibn al-Qayyim]] with saying he wished to bring them out of their own wish into God's wish. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain, and the saying is credited to Ibn al-Qayyim with no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:14:00}
- He describes [[practice/wudu|ablution]] as related of the Prophet: washing the hands three times, rinsing the mouth, sniffing water, washing the face and the arms to the elbows, wiping the head and ears and washing the feet to the ankles, and relates by sense that he said this is my ablution and that of the prophets before me, so whoever adds or subtracts has done wrong. (needs_source: the description and the report are by sense and without a chain, and the narrator is garbled in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:15:30}
- He relates by sense the report of the man whom the Prophet told three times to repeat his prayer until he taught him to say the opening, recite the Fatiha letter by letter, bow, stand upright, prostrate and be at ease in each. He says the Malikis count reciting the Fatiha and sounding its letters among the pillars of prayer, describes bowing with hands on the knees and the back so level that a vessel placed on it would stay, and relates that Ibn Umar bowed so long that doves settled on his back taking it for a wall. (needs_source: the reports are by sense and without a chain, and the school's ruling is given with no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:18:30}
- He says the heart falls ill when deprived of its food of remembrance and reciting the Qur'an, and that the self rises from commanding evil to self-reproaching, then content, pleased, reassured, inspired and perfect, the perfect self being the Prophet's alone. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-12-53|Yusuf, verse 53]], [[passage/quran-75-2|al-Qiyama, verse 2]] and [[passage/quran-89-27|al-Fajr, verse 27]]. The subject is connected to [[concept/nafs-lawwama|the self-reproaching soul]]. (needs_source: the order of the stages is his own) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:20:30}
- He relates that earlier nations had those who were inspired and that this community has Umar, who while preaching in Medina cried out O Sariya, the mountain, the mountain, and when Ali asked him about it said it came to his mind that the army was threatened by an ambush, then a messenger from the army came months later saying they heard a voice like his, took heed and were saved. He likens it to sending and receiving. (needs_source: the story has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:22:00}
- He says a believer kills what was alive, the self, revives what was dead, the heart, and sees what was absent, the hereafter, and that haste is disliked except in certain things such as repentance, paying debts, praying on time, marrying off a daughter to one whose religion and character please and burying the dead. This is his opinion. (needs_source: his list of matters for haste has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:24:00}
- He relates by sense that a Muslim should not sleep without his will at his head, says the will is not only about money but about who will wash, shroud and pray over him, and says he has told his children to bury him where he dies. He says of the one hundred and thirteen thousand Companions who made the pilgrimage with the Prophet only about twenty-three thousand were buried in Mecca and Medina. This is his opinion. The subject is connected to [[concept/wasiyya-fiqh|the will]]. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain, and the numbers have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:26:00}
- He tells that a man asked an old man where the inhabited part of the town was and was taken to the cemetery, where he was told everyone there comes here and none returns from here to there. He says the believer in the grave dislikes returning to the world as an unborn child would dislike returning to the womb, and that a statistic in Germany found some infants dying in their first month from fear of a nightmare of returning to the womb. (needs_source: the story has no chain, and the statistic attributed to Germany has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:28:00}
- He urges holding oneself to account before being held to account and weighing one's deeds before they are weighed, and mentions elders who wrote down the sins of their day in notebooks, asked forgiveness for them and returned rights to their owners. The subject is connected to [[concept/muhasabat-al-nafs|self-accounting]]. This is his opinion. (needs_source: the saying on self-accounting is unattributed) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:29:30}
- He says there is no relation between age or illness and the angel of death, for many a healthy person has died without treatment and many a sick person has lived long. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:30:00}
- He says a person measures his deeds by the scale of the law so that his evil deed is not made to look fair, then takes up the will to guide and to lead astray, holding that God eases guidance for whoever wants it and eases error for whoever wants it, with the example of Abu Bakr accepting Islam when it was offered and Abu Lahab saying perish you, is it for this you gathered us, after which Surat al-Masad came down. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-111-1|al-Masad, verse 1]]. (needs_source: Abu Lahab's words are given by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:33:30}
- He says Moses brought his people out of darkness into light while Muhammad brought out all people, and that those who do not want guidance mock the believers, mislead people and become devils of mankind. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:34:30}
- He says the heirs after a burial shake the dust of the grave from their clothes and return to divide the estate, that the righteous ask what he sent ahead while the people of the world ask what he left, and lists among lasting deeds a righteous child, a continuing charity and beneficial knowledge. This is his opinion. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:36:00}
- He recounts the story of Joseph's brothers: they thought killing him would clear their father's face for them and make them a righteous people after, their father's heart was not emptied for them after what they did, they came at night weeping with blood on the shirt, and he says they slaughtered a gazelle, whereupon Jacob said their souls had made it seem fair to them and that patience was beautiful. He explains beautiful patience as patience without complaint, as beautiful parting is parting without severing and beautiful pardon is pardon without reproach. See [[passage/quran-12-17|Yusuf, verse 17]], [[passage/quran-12-18|Yusuf, verse 18]], [[passage/quran-15-85|al-Hijr, verse 85]] and [[passage/quran-73-10|al-Muzzammil, verse 10]]. (needs_source: the slaughter of a gazelle is not in the verse and has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:38:30}
- He explains the words that you should not waste yourself in sorrow over them as meaning the Messenger conveys and reminds and is not a warden over them, and advises that one who sees his son not praying should pray before him and go to the mosque as an example, saying a devout son spreading his mat before his parents, or a daughter adjusting her veil beside her mother, is a courteous invitation. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-18-6|al-Kahf, verse 6]], [[passage/quran-88-21|al-Ghashiya, verse 21]] and [[passage/quran-88-22|al-Ghashiya, verse 22]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:41:00}
- He mentions that Quraysh asked God to rain stones on them if this was the truth and said that if they followed the guidance with the Prophet they would be snatched from their land while knowing he was on guidance, and recalls the covenant God took from the children of Adam, am I not your Lord, they said yes. He explains disbelief as covering the natural disposition and likens the atheist to a child who hides behind a chair and says I am not here. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-8-32|al-Anfal, verse 32]], [[passage/quran-28-57|al-Qasas, verse 57]] and [[passage/quran-7-172|al-A'raf, verse 172]]. The subject is connected to [[concept/fitra|the natural disposition]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:43:30}
- He closes saying God made the lawful and the unlawful clear and left people on a white road whose night is like its day and from which only the lost turns away. This is his opinion. (needs_source: the last wording is of a report by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep20 00:44:00}

## Episode 21: the expanding of the chest and the hardening of the heart
- He pauses at the verse of Surat al-Zumar on one whose chest God has opened to Islam so that he is on light from his Lord, and woe to those whose hearts are hardened against the remembrance of God. He notes that the surah describes the pious driven to Paradise in groups and the unbelievers to the fire in groups, and says the gates of Paradise stay open for its people while the gates of the fire are bolted on its people because in the world they closed the doors of their hearts, ears and eyes. This is his explanation. See [[passage/quran-39-22|al-Zumar, verse 22]], [[passage/quran-39-71|al-Zumar, verse 71]] and [[passage/quran-39-73|al-Zumar, verse 73]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:03:00}
- He counts as the first cause of a chest not opening the neglect of the prayers at their times, then their lack of humility and calm and the neglect of the voluntary prayers that are like gardens around them. He says a heart that is humble in prayer becomes heavenly so the devils cannot come near it, as bats cannot bear a dazzling light, and that faith and sincerity have a scent the angels love. The subject is connected to [[concept/nawafil|voluntary prayers]]. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:05:30}
- He quotes some scholars as saying that one who persistently leaves the Sunna draws a rebuke from the Prophet that would make the flesh of the face drop, and relates by sense that the Prophet likened himself and those around him to a man who lit a fire while moths and locusts rushed at it and he held them back by their waists and they slipped from him. (needs_source: the scholars are not named, and the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:07:00}
- He says Adam was allowed everything in Paradise and forbidden one tree, which shows that the forbidden things are few and the permitted many, that the exception with except comes after the many, and that those who do not keep the religion accuse the devout of forbidding everything. The subject is connected to [[person/adam|Adam]]. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-2-35|al-Baqara, verse 35]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:09:00}
- He says Satan deceived Adam as he deceives his children, putting poison in honey, and that Joseph's brothers built righteousness on killing Joseph. He tells that one of Adam's sons let his self lead him to kill his brother, so God sent a raven scratching the ground to show him how to bury his brother's body and he became one of the regretful, saying he wanted a beautiful wife and God accepted his brother's offering. He relates by sense that whoever starts an evil practice bears its burden and the burden of those who follow it. See [[passage/quran-35-5|Fatir, verse 5]], [[passage/quran-5-30|al-Ma'ida, verse 30]] and [[passage/quran-5-31|al-Ma'ida, verse 31]]. (needs_source: the motive given for the killing is not in the verse and has no source, and the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:12:00}
- He says sins are of two kinds: a sin that dies with its doer, such as drinking or drugs, and a sin that outlives him, such as a woman who repented and opened a dance school, whose evil deeds are counted against her as long as it stands. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:12:30}
- He says the sign of an opened chest is that God makes obedience and repentance dear to the servant and sin hateful, and opens doors of good he never thought of, while to one He loves He closes the doors of sin, giving the example of a man who sat to do wrong when his mother died so his night became a night of condolence, or whose venue was raided, or who had an accident. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:15:00}
- He says the word lawful is said with an open mouth while unlawful is said with the lips closed, and gives the example of a father angry at a young man peeping at his window who welcomes him when he comes to ask for his daughter at the door, noting that God commanded entering houses by their doors, and distinguishing males from men. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:18:00}
- He says adulterated, incomplete religiosity is more dangerous to the community than open atheism because it is a trial for people, and tells that Umar distributed a garment to each person and, tall as he was, spoke in two garments, so a Companion, Sa'd in the transcript, objected; Umar's son Abdallah answered that he had given his own garment to his father, the man said now we sit and listen, and Umar smiled and said there is no good in you if you do not say it and none in us if we do not hear it. (needs_source: the report has no chain and the name stands as in the machine transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:20:00}
- He says life is a burden to some people and some people are a burden to life, that a sign of an opened chest is that its owner knows no road to lying, and that the liar's calamity is that he believes no one. This is his opinion. (needs_source: the saying on life and people is unattributed) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:21:30}
- He addresses those who want to put out the candles of the believers, by which they light the way for people, saying they will stay in the dark because they have no light, that the believers do not warn anyone but only remind, and that God refuses except to complete His light. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-9-32|al-Tawba, verse 32]], [[passage/quran-24-35|al-Nur, verse 35]] and [[passage/quran-88-21|al-Ghashiya, verse 21]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:23:30}
- He says stones in the road are obstacles to the lowly of aspiration and stairs to the high of aspiration, that God subjected the universe to people and not people to the universe, yet people became slaves to the world so that the slave of the dinar and of the dirham is wretched, and cites lines saying the fault is in the eye and not the sun. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-45-13|al-Jathiya, verse 13]]. (needs_source: the lines are unattributed, and the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:26:30}
- He credits [[person/ibn-qayyim-al-jawziyya|Ibn al-Qayyim]] with saying, when asked about his teacher [[person/ibn-taymiyya|Ibn Taymiyya]], that he subdued desire until it avoided him, relates by sense that Satan never meets Umar on a road without taking another, and relates that Iblis told his followers he would make desire fair to the community of Muhammad so they would not repent of it. He says desire makes a person's evil look good to him, and mentions God's words about one who takes his own desire as his god. See [[passage/quran-45-23|al-Jathiya, verse 23]]. The subject is connected to [[concept/hawa|desire]]. (needs_source: the saying credited to Ibn al-Qayyim, the report and Iblis's words have no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:29:30}
- He lists causes of a hard heart: following desire, many sins and little repentance, eating the unlawful, making light of sins, not seeing truth as truth and falsehood as falsehood, ill treatment of others, cheating in dealings and lying. The subject is connected to [[concept/tatfif-short-measure|cheating in measure]] and [[concept/qaswat-al-qalb-wa-amradih|hardness of the heart]]. See [[passage/quran-83-1|al-Mutaffifin, verse 1]]. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:30:30}
- He credits Umar with saying that no one finds arrogance in himself except from a lowliness he feels within, and credits Abu Bakr with asking God to strengthen his back and not lighten his load. (needs_source: both sayings are credited with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:31:30}
- He says some people attend the Friday sermon with an absent heart, advises one who finds hardness in his heart to look at his Syrian brothers in shelters and the Iraqis being killed, and if his heart is not moved then it is hard, and to soften it with five things: much seeking forgiveness, reading and hearing the Qur'an, supplication, relieving the distress of Muslims and remembering the hereafter. This is his opinion. (needs_source: the five things are his own) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:34:00}
- He says one who eats the unlawful and feeds it to his children has blessing removed from them and the child grows up neither treating the lawful as lawful nor the forbidden as forbidden, and that a person should recall the hereafter and review his accounts. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:35:00}
- He relates by sense that the Prophet said no one enters Paradise by his deeds, and when they asked not even you, said not even I unless God covers me with His mercy. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:36:00}
- He says Wayl is a valley in Hell from which the fire seeks refuge fourteen times a day, distinguishes those who are heedless in their prayer from those heedless of their prayer, credits Ibn Abbas with praising God that He said of and not in their prayer, and relates by sense that a servant has of his prayer only what he was aware of. See [[passage/quran-107-4|al-Ma'un, verse 4]] and [[passage/quran-107-5|al-Ma'un, verse 5]]. (needs_source: the count is credited to unnamed scholars, the saying is credited to Ibn Abbas with no source, and the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:38:30}
- He advises one who wants to concentrate in prayer to read a page or two of the Qur'an before it, likening this to warming a car's engine and an athlete's warm-up before a match and to wearing clothes suited to each setting, and says the heart should be made ready to meet God. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep21 00:41:00}

## Episode 22: the compassionate Messenger and the understanding of judgement
- He pauses at the last two verses of Surat al-Tawba, on a messenger coming from among themselves, grievous to him is what hurts them, anxious for them, to the believers kind and merciful. He says Ibn Abbas read the word for themselves in another form, relates by sense that the Prophet was never given a choice between two matters without choosing the easier, and judges him the easiest jurist in history and his earliest Companions the easiest after him. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-9-128|al-Tawba, verse 128]] and [[passage/quran-9-129|al-Tawba, verse 129]]. (needs_source: the reading is credited to Ibn Abbas with no source, and the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:03:00}
- He explains grievous to him is what hurts you as meaning your hardship weighs on him, and anxious for you as intense concern, and puts in the Prophet's mouth that no member of his community complains of a thorn without his feeling its pain in his heart. (needs_source: the words are credited to the Prophet with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:03:30}
- He says God is every day engaged in some matter, freeing the captive, answering the caller, healing the sick, giving the asker, forgiving the sin, raising some and lowering others, and gives as example those circling the Kaaba speaking hundreds of languages without the number of requests burdening God. See [[passage/quran-55-29|al-Rahman, verse 29]]. (needs_source: the first report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:05:00}
- He says God told His Prophet to be patient for his Lord, understands patience as being in performing obligations, under harm from people and in what God gives, and says the first blessing after Islam and the sending of the Prophet is the Qur'an. He tells the report likening the reader of the Qur'an to the citron, good in taste and scent, the believer who does not read to the date, good in taste with no scent, the hypocrite who reads to the basil, fragrant but bitter, and the hypocrite who does not read to the colocynth, bitter with no scent, and credits it to al-Bukhari. See [[passage/quran-74-7|al-Muddaththir, verse 7]]. (needs_source: the report is by sense, and the attribution to al-Bukhari is given with no wording or location) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:08:00}
- He urges one not to be bitter nor make people's lives bitter, and says he wants individuals, groups and governments on God's way. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:09:00}
- He tells that Abd al-Malik ibn Marwan told his people they wanted him to rule them by the way of Abu Bakr and Umar but did not wish to be like their subjects, and that a man asked Ali why no strife occurred under Abu Bakr and Umar but did under Uthman and him, and he answered that the subjects of Abu Bakr and Umar were like Uthman and his like, while his subjects and Uthman's were like the questioner. (needs_source: both reports have no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:10:00}
- He says he wants governments half revelation and half awareness, for those with revelation and no awareness harm wisdom, and those with awareness and no revelation are adrift and cut off from the teachings of heaven. This is his statement. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:10:30}
- He explains that al-Fatiha teaches one not to choose between those who earned anger and those astray but to follow the path of those God favoured, the prophets, the truthful, the martyrs and the righteous. This is his explanation. See [[passage/quran-1-6|al-Fatiha, verse 6]], [[passage/quran-1-7|al-Fatiha, verse 7]] and [[passage/quran-4-69|al-Nisa, verse 69]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:11:30}
- He says the believer who follows the Prophet keeps away from idle talk, mentions that parliaments strike offensive speech from their records, and says he bears no false witness, adding that a father may give false witness for his son saying my son does not err, whereas every child of Adam errs and no one enters Paradise by his deeds. This is his opinion. See [[passage/quran-25-72|al-Furqan, verse 72]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:13:00}
- He recites God's description of Muhammad and those with him as firm against unbelievers, merciful among themselves, bowing and prostrating, and their likeness as a plant that sends out its shoots, strengthens, thickens and stands on its stalk. He explains it as qualitative balance of abilities and mentions God's promise to the believers of succession, empowerment and fear replaced by security. This is his explanation. See [[passage/quran-48-29|al-Fath, verse 29]] and [[passage/quran-24-55|al-Nur, verse 55]]. (needs_source: the explanation as qualitative balance is his own) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:14:30}
- He quotes a saying that if you want to show God's favour to you recall what is from Him to you, and if you want the way of seeking forgiveness and repentance recall what is from you to Him. (needs_source: the saying is unattributed) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:15:00}
- He relates by sense that people heard a cry at night in Medina and went out to find the Prophet returning on a bare-backed horse saying do not be afraid. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:16:00}
- He explains the verse that if they turn away say God is enough for me, relates by sense that the Kursi next to the Throne is like a ring thrown in a desert, says the Throne, Kursi, Tablet and Pen are called in creed the heard-of matters that must be believed though unseen, and says Moses stayed thirty years in Pharaoh's palace to study the notables. See [[passage/quran-2-3|al-Baqara, verse 3]]. The subject is connected to [[concept/tawakkul|reliance on God]]. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain, and the thirty years has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:17:30}
- He tells that when some Arabs wished to withhold zakat after the Prophet's death they cited the verse take from their wealth a charity and pray for them, so [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] prepared eleven armies commanded by [[person/khalid-ibn-al-walid|Khalid ibn al-Walid]], and that the Prophet had prepared an expedition against the Byzantines under [[person/usama-ibn-zayd|Usama]], seventeen years old, with Abu Bakr and Umar among its soldiers. When the Companions objected, Abu Bakr said he had come as a follower and not an innovator, and walked beside Usama riding though he was sixty-one, asking leave to keep Umar in Medina. The subject is connected to [[event/expedition-of-usama|the expedition of Usama]] and [[event/ridda-wars|the Ridda wars]]. See [[passage/quran-9-103|al-Tawba, verse 103]]. (needs_source: the numbers, ages and Abu Bakr's walking and asking leave have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:23:00}
- He says an environment in which the elderly and scholars are not respected and the weak not shown mercy is a dead one, relates by sense that he is not of us who does not honour our elder, show mercy to our young and know our scholar's right, and says we do not know scholars their rights, restrict them, forbid good speech and turn religion into a trade. This is his opinion. (needs_source: the report is by sense and without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy mawkib-al-nour/ep22 00:22:00}
- He tells of David and Solomon judging the field into which a people's sheep strayed: David told the field's owner to take the flock, while Solomon, fifteen and waiting at the door out of courtesy to his father, held that the owner should take the flock's milk and the flock's owner replant the field until it was as it had been. God praised both as given judgement and knowledge and made Solomon understand. See [[passage/quran-21-78|al-Anbiya, verse 78]] and [[passage/quran-21-79|al-Anbiya, verse 79]].
